Copv OF LETTER.
26th September, 1912.
DEAR SIR,
I have to state for the information of the Louth
County Council that the Department have had a further
report made in respect of the quayat Dullany. The general
condition of the place do not seem favourable for any
large expenditure from the Department funds, but it
is now understood that the County Council are pre-
pared to interest themselves in the matter. The De-
partment would be prepared to reconsider the question
of making contribution in conjunction with the County
Council for the purpose of providing an extension of
the quay in question. It is suggested that this should
take the form of a concrete groyne, the cost of which
is estimated at about 1500. The Department are there-
fore willing to contribute within a limit of {250 one
half of the actual cost involved, provided the other half
be forthcoming locally.
I am, dear Sir,
Vour obedient servant,
T. P. GILL
Resolved—That we strongly recommend the Louth
County Council to consent to the offer of the Depart-
ment of Agriculture, and agree to bear one half of the
cost of repairs to Dunany Pier—cost not to exceed {250.
Same to be a County-at-large charge, including Urban
Districts.

COUNTY SURVEYOR'S OFFICE, DUNDALK,
28th November, 1912.
To the County Council (Supplemental Report).
MR. CHAIRMAN AND beg to report
that the extension of the Electric Lighting System by the
Dundalk Urban Council to St. Patrick's Cemetery,
Dowdallshill, necessitating the erection of poles along
the footpath may diminish to some extent the ad-
vantage to be derived from the widening specified in
proposal No. 7 Dundalk Rural District now before you.
The poles have been erected in the usual way along the
outer edge of the footpath. When the widening speci-
fied has been carried out, 2ft. 6 ins. of the footpath
will project outside the poles. Poles without lights
14
so near the middle of the footpath may cause an
obstruction. I may add that several lamps attached
to poles near the town are a decided boon to persons
living in the Rural District. In considering this matter,
if proposal No. 7 were rejected. other arlangement*
might be mpde

J. M. C. LVONS.

Proposed by Mr. Hamill, J .P.,
Seconded by Mr. Wm. A. Doran, J.P.
Unanimously resolved—(l) That we condemn in the
strongest possible manner the tyrannical action of the
National Board in summarily dismissing Mr. Mansfield,
Vice-President of the Teachers' Organization, for his
public condemnation of the system of inspection in the
National Schools.
17
(2) That we call upon the Chief Secretary for Ireland
to secure a thorough investigation into the working of
the inspection system under the Board of National
Education, to include in this investigation the dismissal
of Mr. Mansfield, to produce the correspondence between
himself and the National Board on said dismissal, and
to ensure that the personnel of the Commission shall
be one commanding the confidence of the Teachers
and the Public. Copies of this resolution to be sent
to the Chief Secretary for Ireland, The Commissioners
of National Education, The M. P.s for North and South
Louth, Daily; Independent, and Mr. E. Mansfield.
DUNDALK GAELIC LEAGUE.
have been asked by above Committee to
request Louth County Council to give an expression of
opinion on proposed Birrell grant to pupils of primary
schools. Will you kindly make reference to this point
on agenda, if not too late.
P. M'DONNELL, Secretary.
Proposed by Mr. Hamill, J.P.,
Seconded by Mr. Kelly.
That this Council endorses the attitude taken
by the General Council of County Councils on the
scheme for Scholarships for pupils of primary
schools by Mr. Augustine Birrell, and that we decline
to relinquish our legal rights in the manner suggested
by the said scheme, having already declared Irish an
essential subject for Scholarships in the National
University of Ireland.
Amendment—
Proposed by Mr. Wm. A. Doran, J.P.,
Seconded by Mr. J. H. M' Ardle.
That we proceed to the next business on the agenda.
On a division there voted for the amendment—
Messrs. Wm. A. Doran, Duffy, Wynne, P. Byrne,
P. Kearney and M 'Ardle—6.
Against the amendment—Messrs. Connolly, Hamill,
O'Connell, P. Woods, Kelly, M 'Carthy—6. The Chair-
man and Mr. Dolan did not vote.
The voting being equal the Chairman put the original
motion to a vote, with same result.
CORRESPONDENCE.
LOCAL GOVERNMENT BOARD, DUBLIN,
25th November, 1912.
SIR,—With reference to your letter of the 15th inst.
enclosing a resolution of the Louth County Council
proposing to rescind the order requiring Rate Collectors
to lodge the full amount of their warrants each half-
year, I am directed by the Local Government Board
for Ireland to draw attention to the terms of the Public
Bodies Order, 1904, the terms of which are mandatory.
If the Collectors in Co. Louth are not prepared to
carry out their duties in accordance with that order,
they should resign their positions, as exemptions cannot
be made in their favour.
I am, sir,
Vour obedient servant,
J. E. DEVLIN,
Assistant Secretary.
19
LOCAL GOVERNMENT BOARD, DUBLIN,
27th November, 1912.
am directed by the Local Government Board
for Ireland to acknowledge the receipt of your letter
of the 23rd inst. relative to the proposed provisional
scheme for the treatment of persons in Louth County
suffering from Tuberculosis, and, in reference thereto,
I am to state that the Board observe that the cost of
the twelve beds at Peamount Sanatorium, which the
County Council propose to purchase is estimated at {70
per bed. The Board think it well to point out that this
figure is to be regarded at present as an approximate
estimate, and that the ultimate liability of the County
Council cannot be determined until the Institution has
been completed.
As regards the revenue account in connection with
the scheme, I am to point out that it would be well that
the County Council should ascertain from the County
Insurance Committee what proportion of the sum
available for Sanatorium benefit will be placed at the
disposal of the Council for meeting the expenses of the
scheme.
The Board desire to add that they have been advised
that an Insurance Committee are only empowered to
make arrangement with persons or local authorities
having the management of a Sanatorium or other
Institution for the treatment of Tuberculosis, con-
iequently the item relating to the maintenance of
Insured persons in Peamount Sanatorium will require
to be modified.
I am, sir,
Vour obedient servant,
J. E. DEVLIN,
Assistant Secretary.
20
NATIONAL HEALTH INSURANCE COMMISSION,
DUBLIN,
27th Nov., 1912.
SIR,—The National Health Insurance Commission
(Ireland) desire to state, for the information of the
County of Louth Insurance Committee, that they have
had before them a copy of a Scheme for the treatment
of cases of Tuberculosis in the County which is to come
up for discussion at the meeting of the Committee on
the 28th inst., with a view to its being recommended
to the County Council for adoption. The Commission
observe that, according to the statement in the Scheme
setting forth the estimated annual receipts and expen-
diture, a credit balance of 1884 would be available each
year, and they would therefore suggest to the Com-
mittee that this sum might with advantage be allocated
as follows :—
(a) In the provision of a whole time nurse, with an
allowance for travelling expenses.
(b) In the appointment of a Clerk in connection with
the proposed Central Dispensary, at a wage of say LI
a week, and (c) in supplementing the amount (L 100)
already set aside for domiciliary treatment, as that
amount is totally inadequate for the purpose. The
Commission would add that it is not practicable at the
present time to frame a reliable estimate of the amount
that would be required to meet the annual cost Of
domiciliary treatment.
I am, sir,
Vour obedient servant,
JOHN HULIHAN.
21

SECRETARY'S OFFICE, COURTHOUSE,
DUNDALK,
16th December, 1912.
DEAR SIR,—I am directed by the Louth County
Insurance Committee to inform you that they have had
under their consideration the Scheme proposed by the
County Council for the treatment of Tuberculosis in
23
the County, and with reference thereto, they beg to
submit the following proposals to the Council for their
consideration.
(I) That the sum of one shilling and two pence per
insured person be handed to the County Council for the
treatment of Tubercular Insured Person and their
dependants.
(2) To expunge the words
1 Clerk for Insurance
Committee, etc., {75," and to insert " 1 Clerk for Central
Dispensary, {50.'
(3) That two Nurses and one Servant be procured for
Central Dispensary—principal, 180, Assistant, 140, and
Servant {10.
(4) That 125 of the {75 originally suggested as salary
for Clerk be devoted to Nurses travelling expenses.
(5) That the credit balance of the entire Scheme
should be devoted to Domiciliary and other forms of
treatment necessary.
I am, Sir,
Yours faithfully,
VAL WARREN, Secretary.
Proposed by Mr. Bernard Hamill, J.P.,
Seconded by Mr. Wm. Woods, J.P., and
Resolved—That the Scheme for the treatment of the
Tuberculosis Population of County Louth as provision-
ally adopted by the County Council be arnended by the
exclusion therein of the recommendation as set forth
in the letter of the County Insurance Committee, dated
the 16th day of December, 1912.
NATIONAL HEALTH INSURANCE COMMISSION,
DUBLIN,
7th January, 1913.
County Louth Insurance Committee.
SIR,—Adverting to your letter of the 4th ultimo cyn
the subject, I am directed by the National Health
Insurance Commission (Ireland) to state, for the infor-
mation of the Louth County Council, that Mr. Richard
Mathews, Knockdillon, Dunleer, has been notified that

I am, Sir,

PIERCE KENT, Assistant Secretary.

OFFICE OF PUBLIC WORKS,
9th December, 1912.
SIR,—In reply to your inquiry of the 20th ultimo, on
the subject of the Gyles' Quay Coastguard Station,
I am directed by the Commissioners of Public Works
to inform you that this Station was closed and surren-
dered by the Admiralty to the Landlord in 1905. The
boat slip is not included in the premises so surrendered ;
it was built at the joint expense of the Crown and the
Royal National Lifebcat Institution about 1877.
I am to add that certain pier and harbour works, viz. ,
a pier consisting of awgarf, a storm parapet, and sloping
escapement, etc., with excavation of the harbour, were
executed by the Board under the Sea Fisheries Act,
and transferred to the Grand Jury in 1879. So far as
the Board's records shows, the boat slip in question
was not included in the transfer, but if the County
Council desire it to be transferred to them now, the
Board for their part will be glacl to facilitate the Council
in the matter.
I am, Sir,
Vour obedient servant,
H. WILLIAMS, Secretary.
December 16, 1912.
DEAR MR. reference to the resolution
of the County Council to refund the amount of Rates
paid by the Fund on Dun Dealgan for the year before
we could secure its exemption, I understand that the
Local Government Board have at present refused to
sanction the refund on the grounds that rates once
paid cannot be refunded.
I cannot think that this is either right cr just. The
rates are demanded with strongest penalties for non-
payment. We paid without prejudice, being assured
that . as soon as we had an opportunity of laying the
business, etc., before the valuation authority we could
get back what we had been called on to pay for property
used for public purposes and benefits.
The Council know the circtllnstances, and the good
object, and also, I hope, the great and to a large extent,
I think, successful exertions the Archæological Society
have made to repair and restore the old Irish fort and
to get together a collection of historical objects of all
periods and all interests.
I am aware of my own knowledge that in similar
circumstances, where there was a question of a reduction
of valuation, other bodies have refunded the amount
overpaid. In our case the circumstances are more in
our favour, and the amount (15 odd) although small,
is of considerable importance to the fund, which is very
small. Will you kindly bring the matter once more
before the Council, whose consideration we are grateful
for, and trust that they may authorise you to issue tlle
refund.
Yours faithfully,
HARRY TEMPEST,
Hon. Sec. Dun Dealgan Fund.
Referred to the County Council.

CORPORATION OF DROGHEDA.
TowN CLERK'S OFFICE,
COURTHOUSE, DROGHEDA
18th December, 1912.
DEAR SIR,—On every Saturday and Fair Day
Mr. Hughes, the County Secretary for Agriculture,
uses a room in this Courthouse, which necessitates a
fire being lighted, and on every Thursday and Friday
Agricultural Classes are conducted necessitating both
lighting and heating ; under the existing arrangements
the Corporation are asked to contribvlte one-tuird of
the cost, and as you know they have no power, strictly
speaking, to incur any such expenditure. Would it not
be well, in order to avoid the possibility of a sur-
charge, if sonle contribution were made from the funds
of the County Scheme for Agriculture in which the
County Council and the Corporation would participate,
Although the possibility of a surcharge may seem re-
mote, yet in my experience, one never knows what
question will be raised at audit upon the complaint of
some individual or another who is always on the look
out for something to complain about.
Vours faithfully,
J. B. CONNOLLY.
36
WOMEN'S NATIONAL HEALTH ASSOCIATION OF IRELAND.
VICE REGAL LODGE,
DUBLIN,
December 20th, 1912.
DEAR SIR,—At the meeting of the representatives of
County Councils sending patients to Peamount, held
at Ely House, on Thursday, December 5th, to confer
on the basis to be adopted for the formation of Com-
mittees of Management for Peamount and Rossclare,
several alternatives were discussed and also the question
as to whether Counties making provisional use of the
accommodation at the Women's National Health
Association Sanatoria should be represented on the
Permanent Committee. It was finally decided that
representation should only be given to Counties con-
tracting for permanent beds, and that the Committee
of Management should consist of one representative
from each County Council or County Borough contracting
for permanent beds, and that there be added from the
Women's National Health Association a number equal
to one half of such County representation. This was
moved by Mr. Walter M 'Murrough Kavanagh (Chairman
Carlow County Council), and seconded by Mr. David
M. Moriarity (Chairman Kerry County Council), and
carried unanimously. Certain difficulties arising out
of the legal interpretation of Section 16 of the Insurance
Act were considered, and a letter from Mr. Glynn,
Chairman of the Insurance Commissioners, was read,
saying that he was advised that the best way of pro-
37
ceeding would be " To prepare a perfectly simple and
intelligible tripartite agreement between the Comnlittee
of Management, the County Council, and the Insurance
Committee, whereby the Committee of Management
undertook to treat insured persons in the Sanatorium
in consideration of the payment of the Sanatorium Fund
by the Insurance Committee to the County Council,
and, at the same time, the County Council undertook
to provide for ' the treatment of any insured persons
not treated in the Sanatorium.
If this
agreement be adopted the legal difficulty will
be got over, and there will be no splitting up of the 1/3
between the Committee of Management, The Insurance
Committee and the County Council.
The meeting also considered two alternatives suggested
by Mr. Glynn. (1) That the agreement above referred
to should form the basis of the agreements between
County Councils which have agreed to take beds in Pea-
mount, and that the Insurance Committees of these
Counties should be parties to the agreements, which
should be made with the Comtnittee of Management of
Peamount ; or (2) That a Joint Board should be formed
under the Tuberculosis Act of 1908 between the Councils
which have taken permanent beds in Peamount and that
the Scheme to be drawn up between these Councils
should provide that for the first three years the Women's
National Health Association, in consideration of the
temporary work to be done in Peamount, over and
above the permanent work undertaken for the County
Councils, should be represented on the Joint Board and
on the Committee of Management. The meeting
38
unanimously decided in favour of the first Scheme,
believing that for the presenf the County Councils did
not wish to assume further liability for Peamount,
although it was agreed by all present that in the event
of any special need, Counties who were represented on
the Committee of Management would favourably consider
any appeal made to them by the Committee.
The Special Council Meeting held by the Women's
National Health Association on the evening of the same
day (Dec. 5th) adopted the recommendation above
quoted as the basis of the Committee of Management,
and nominated as their first six representatives the
following :—
The President (The Countess of Aberdeen), The Hon.
Treasurer (Sir Wm. T. Thompson, M.D.), The Medical
Secretary (Dr. Prudence Gaffikin), Mrs. J. J. Lawlor,
Mrs. Michael F. Cox, Miss M 'Donald (late Matron of the
Richmond Hospital), and that pending the constitution
of this Committee, the present Provisional Committee
of Supervision be requested to carry on the work en-
trusted to them. A deputation from the Meeting of
Representatives of County Councils and the Women's
National Health Association waited on the Local Govern-
ment Board on December 6th and submitted the de-
cision arrived at to the Vice-President. Sir Henry
Robinson explained that whilst there were other modes
Of forming Conjoint Committees of Management, either
under the Tuberculosis Act of 1908, or under the In-
surance Act of 1911 which would require the sanction
of the Local Government Board, the plan stlbmitted
39
by the deputation required no such sanction, but left
the responsibility of maintenance on the Women's
National Health Association and the Committee of
Management, whereas the other two plans would carry
responsibility for maintenance on the part of the con-
tracting County Councils. The deputation made it
clear that the representative meeting the day before
had decided against assuming any such responsibility
at present, and it was pointed out that the action now
adopted would not prevent a Conjoint Board being
formed later on. Sir Henry Robinson agreed with this
conclusion, and the deputation thanked him and with-
drew.
I beg, therefore, to ask you to request your County
Council at their next meeting to nominate a represen-
tative to act on the Committee of Management of the
Sanatorium where your Council has contracted for beds.
I remain,
Vours very faithfully,
COUNTESS OF ABERDEEN.

ALBOROUGH HousE, DUBLIN,

• Postmaster General, actillg under the
powers conferred upon him by the Telegraph Acts, 1863
to 1908, is desirous of obtaining the consent of the Louth

of which are as follows :—Erection of overhead tele-

CORRESPONDENCE.
CLERMONT PARK
DUNDALK,
13th January, 1913.
SIR,—A pony belonging to me took ill about 10 days
ago, and sorne of the men about the place told the
Sergeant at Blackrock. He calne to see it ; he spoke
to me and told me it was the mange it had, and com-
pelled me to get it shot that evening, Thursday last
9th inst.
It was the only little pony I had, and it places
me at a great inconvenience to be without one.
I hope
you may be able to get me the value of it, which was
I am sure about 15 or 61, so that I may be able to get
one very soon which I cannot do without.
An answer as soon as is convenient will oblige.
Vours respectfully,
LIZZIE CALLAN.
No order made.

DROGHEDA RURAL SUB-COMMITTEE OF THE Co. Loti'1'Y1

L)EAR SIR,—Mr. Charles Markey, J. P. , Glaspistol
Clogherhead, having resigned his membership of this

appointment in his place of Mr. Laurence Murphy, D.C.,

L0CAL GOVERNMENT BOARD, DUBLIN,
17th January, 1913.
TUBERCULOSIS SCHEMES.
Financial Arrangements.
SIR,——I am directed by the Local Government Board
for Ireland to state that they learn from their Medical
Inspectors who have been in communication with
County Councils on the subject of the schemes which
are in course of preparation for the establishment of
Dispensaries and Sanatoria, that while the Councils
are fully alive to their powers under Part 11. of the
Tuberculosis Prevention (Ireland) Act, 1908, they are
reluctant to bind themselves to any final financial
undertakings in connection with Sanatorium benefit
under the National Insurance Act until they have some
more definite idea of the burdens in respect of main-
tenance and administration expenses for which they
may render the ratepayers annually liable if they enter
into arrangements with Insurance Committees for
institutional or domiciliary treatment of insured persons
and their dependants suffering from tuberculosis.
The Board, therefore, deem it well to explain, for
the information of the County Councils, the Insurance
Committees, and the County Councils in regard to the
maintenance charges which may be imposed by the
provision of treatment for persons suffering from tuber-
culosis in each county.
This is clearly set out in a letter from the Chancellor
of the Exchequer to Mr. Hobhouse on the 31st July last,
in which the following paragraph occurs which covers
the case of Ireland
' Under the Insurance Act an annual sum of about one
million is provided for the treatment of insured persons While
the Bill was passing through Parliament provisions were inserted
for extending sanatorium benefit to dependants of insured,
and in view of this the Government consented to bear one-half
of any deficit in regard to sanatorium benefit where local
authorities undertook the other half.
It is now urged that
schemes for the treatment of tuberculosis should relate to the
whole community, and that generally they should be organised
and carried out by the councils of counties and county boroughs.
This extension involves additional outlay, and in view of this
the Government have decided to place at the disposal of the
Local Government Boards of the three kingdoms annuallv a
sum of money which will represent approximately half -the
total estimated cost of treating the non-insured persons as well
'I'his money will be
as the dependants of insured persons.
distributed by the Local Government Boards, in pursuance of
regulations to be made by those Departments, to local authorities
which undertake schemes, to be approved by the Departments,
for the general treatment of tuberculosis in their areas ; and
provision will be made accordingly for these grants in the
Estimates of the three departments.
As rggards the cost of treating insured persons, the sum
already provided under the Insurance Act, which, as I have
already stated, is about one million pounds, can only pass to
local authorities in pursuance of agreements made between
them and insurance committees. But I have no doubt that
insurance committees generally will be anxious to deal with the
local authority of their area, and the Association may rest
assured that the G vernment will do all in their power to secure
this. "
The annual sum of one million above referred to as
being provided for the treatment of insured persons
represents the estimated produce of the contribution of
IS. 3d. from insured persons ullder Section 16 (2) of the
National Insurance Act. Ireland's share of this sum
will accordingly depend upon the number of insured
personk in each ecuntv.
The County Councils will also observe from the fore-
going letter that not only does the guarantee of the
Treasury extend to payment of one-half of the net
cost of the treatment of tuberculosis when undertaken
by the County Councils by arrangement with Insurance
Committees on behalf of insured persons and their
dependants, after deducting the amount contributed
by the Insurance Cornmittee, but, furthermore, where
Irish County Councils have taken or take measures
under the tuberculosis Prevention (Ireland) Act for the
treatment of the disease among the inhabitants of the
county generally, whether insured persons or otherwise,
6
the whole of the net expenses chargeable upon the rates
under the Act may be pooled and onerhalf of the total
sum incurred with the Board's sanction may be de-
frayed from the Treasury Maintenance Grant provided
the statutory procedure is observed.
The County Councils will see at a glance that this is
an enormous concession, and one which they should be
quick to take advantage of by pressing forward the
completion of their schemes for Dispensaries and Sana-
toria which have been before them for the last few
months, and also by making arrangements with the
Instlrance Committees for the treatment of insured
persons and their dependants.
The Councils will, of course, understand that this
grant-in-aid is an annual subsidy and quite apart fronl
and in addition to the 1145,ooo provided under the
Finance Act, 1911, for the establishment of Sanatoria
and Dispensaries now about to be distributed in ac-
cordance with the principles laid down in the Board's
circular of 27th August last.
At the same time, it will be noted that the two
grants though distinct are connecting links in the
Government scheme, the {145,000 being applicable to
the capital cost, and the annual grant defraying one-
half of the net cost in respect of the salaries of officers,
establishment expenses and upkeep of the institutions
and the treatment of patients therein, for which the
County Councils would be liable.
In preparing their estimate of their probable liabili-
ties the County Council would no doubt be able to obtain
from the Annual Reports of the Registrar-General
some statistics of the number of cases of tuberculosis in
each county which might form a basis for their estimate
of the year's expenditure. The Board also understand
that at an early date the Insurance Commissioners may
be able to give them an estimate of the amount of the
Insurance fund which may be available in each county.
The National Insurance Act requires that Insurance
7
Committees should be primarily respormsible for seeing
that insured persons, and any class of the dependants
of insured persons to whom they have extended sana-
torium benefit, receive the appropriate treatment,
and this responsibility necessarily remains under the
system now proposed.
Subject, however, to this responsibility, it is competent
for the Insurance Committees, with the consent of the
Insurance Comlllissioners. to enter into arrangements
with local authorities for providing treatment and to
pay over the sums available under the Act for tlle
purposes of such treatment. The Council will observe
that the Chancellor of the Exchequer in his letter to
Mr. Hobhouse expressed the opinion that the Insurance
Committees would be anxious to deal witll the local
authority of their areas and he undertook that the
Government would do all in their power to secure that
this should be done.
Having regard to all the foregoing considerations
and to the immeasurable benefits which may accrue
to persons suffering from tuberculosis in Ireland by
taking the fullest advantage of the Treasury Grant,
the County Councils would, in the Board's opinion,
be well advised to levy a rate of ld. in the Pound for
the coming year's expenditure under the Tuberculosis
Act.
I am, Sir,
Vour obedient servant,
J. E. DEVLIN, Assistant Sec.
A deputati011 consisting of Messrs. Hughes and Peter
M'l)onnell, Rate Collectors, waited 011 the Committee
relative to the followillg letter from the Local Goverll-
ment Board.

LOCAL GOVERMNENT BOARD, DUBLIN,
18th December, 1912.
SIR,—With reference to the lists of arrears of rate
in the County of Louth for the period ended the 30th
of September, 1912, recently forwarded by you to this
Department, I am directed by the Local Government
Board for Ireland to return the list, herewith, and to
state that the Rulings of the Board are duly stamped
thereon.
In the case of item 2426 on Collector John Kelly's list,
the Board assume that the Rate in the case of No. 2427
has been duly collected and lodged.
Item 165 on Collector Patrick M 'Cullough's lists has
been excepted from the Board's approval, the receipt
in this case having been altered. The Board have also
disallowed 2s. 6d. the cost of a decree included in the
amount of 5s. 7d. appearing ill column 12 opposite item
2793 on Collector Patrick M'L)onnelI's lists. Such
charges should not appear 011 the Schedules of Uncol-
lected Rates.
Item 5381 on Collector John Wood's lists has also been
excepted from approval.
Fuller information regarding
the period of vacancy all(l the present occupiers, if any,
should be given in this case.
With reference to Collector M 'Cullough's second list
(amount 142 12s. cod ) the Board desire Ille to point out
that if, as stated in your communication of the 29th
ultimo, the Estate has been sc;ld in Court, it would appear
that on proper representations being made the Collector
would no doubt be paid the amount of rates and arrears
due, which, from the information before the Board,
appears to be a personal debt due to the Collector and
recoverable as such by him.
The Board presume that the original receipts for
these rates are still in existence and in your possession
9
or in that of the Collector. In the former eventuality
the receipts might be handed tc the Collector to enable
him to obtain payment.
The Board have also excepted from their approval,
pending further satisfactory explanation, the several
items appearing throughout the lists in regard to which
no receipts have been furnished. With reference to
those cases in which it is stated that the receipts were
given when the portion of rates concerning land was
paid, I am to state that the Board are unable to approve
of these items, as in the absence of the receipts there is no
12 of the lists represent the actual balance remaining
over after the land portion cf the original rating had
been paid.
I am, at the same time, to point out that the Rulings
of the Board have been givetl on the assumption that
the information contained in the several lists is in all
respects accurate, and must not, therefore, be regarded
as interfering with the jurisdiction of the Auditor in
case for any cause he should at audit find it necessary
to deal otherwise with any item or items.
\Vith reference to certain cases through011t the lists,
e.g., items 4809, 4860, &c., on Collector John Wood's
lists in which though part payment of the original rating
appears to have been made yet the original receipt
remains attached to the lists, the Board desire me to
request that the procedure outlined in the last three
paragraphs of their Letter of the 5th of June last may
be followed in future, otherwise the items involved
will be liable to disallowance.
The receipts of Collectors Patrick Hughes and Patrick
Cluskey have been furnished in disorder, and I am to
draw your attention to the last paragraph of the Board's
Letter of the 7th December. 1911, No. 140/M/1911,
and to request that in future all the receipts may be
10
furnished filed in the same order as the corresponding
items appearing on the lists.
The Board observe that many items throughout the
lists are proposed to be struck off as irrecoverable on
account of poverty, and I am to point out that a CCI-
lector is only entitled at the end of the half-year ended
the 30th September to claim a refund in respect of
rates which are strictly irrecoverable. The amounts of
such items therefore should appear in future in column
14 of the lists, so that they may be recovered during
the currency of the second half-year of the yearly rate.
I am also to point out that items respecting vacant
houses appearing in the arrear lists for the half-year
ended the 30th of September should not be struck off
as irrecoverable except where the houses in question
were vacant at the time the rate was made aud remained
unoccupied throughout the whole of the period claimed
for.
A Summary of the enclosed lists should be forthwith
furnished to this Office. A supply of forms is sent
herewith.
I am, Sir,
Vour obedient servant,
J. E. DEVLIN,
Assistant Secretary.

COUNTY LOUTH SCHOLARSHIP
COMMITTEE.
Minutes of Proceedings of lhe above Commütee, held
in the Council Chamber, on Monday, the 20th day of
January, 1913
Present .
In the Chair—Mr. J. Connolly, J . P.
Other Members—I). O'Connell and Jos. T. Dolan.
It was resolved to submit for approval to the County
Council the following Schellie for Scholarships for the
ensuing year :
CONDITIONS OF Lou'l'll COUN'I'V COUNCIL SCHOLARSHIP
SCHEME, 1913.
The Louth County Council offer two Scholarships of
150 a year each, tenable at the I)ublin or other College
of the National University for the Session October,1913
to June, 1914, and renewable for the two succeeding
years, alid for a fourth year if the Student follows a
Course of Agriculture, or in Industrial or Commercial
Science.
'I'he Scholarships will be open for Competition
to male and female Students who fulfil the following
requirements
(1). Whose parents or guardians are and have been
resident in Co. Louth for the past three years,
or whose parents or guardians have at any time
within the last twenty years been resident in
the County for six years.
(2).
(3).
(4).
(5).
The
13
Who satisfy the County Council that they are
in need of assistance—that they are unable of
their own means to obtain a University
Educatio n.
Who have been educated ill Ireland.
Who are of good health and character and
under 19 years of age.
Who pass in Irish and reach a standard of
honours in the aggregate of subjects in the
'Test Examination which gives proof of ability
enough to put a University Educaticn to good
use.
' rest Examination will be the Intermediate
Examination of 1913—the order of merit being deter-
mined by the printed marks obtained by the applicants
in Senior Grade Honours as published ill the Official
Results List—a Pass in Irish being essential.
The Scholarships shall be awarded to the two candi-
dates best qualified in the juclgment of the County
Council on consideration of merit in this test exarni-
nation, and of the fulfilment of other requiremellts.
If either or both of the highest eligible conmpetitors will
not aeeept Scholarships they shall be awarded to those
next in merit, if qualified.
One or both Scholarships Illay be altogether withheld
if sufficient merit or need of assistance is not shown.
The Student accepting a Scholarship must pass the
Matriculation of the National University (unless the
Ulliversity accept his Intermediate Examination as
qualification) in 1913, and attend regularly the first
year's course in some faculty of the Ulliversity, and
reside in a hostel or other residence approved by the
County Council and the College autllorities.
14
The College fees and maintenance shall be paid direct
by the County Council to the College authorities and the
hostel proprietors, the balance, if any, to be expended
on books and other expenses.
In order to retain a Scholarship for the second and
third year a Student must pass the College Examination
of that year at a Stalldard of merit to be fixed by the
Council in consultation with the College authorities,
and must also present a satisfactory report from the
College as to attendance at lectures and general conduct
during the year.
The County Council shall have cotnplete discreti011
in applying or modifying these rules and in withholding
or reducing Scholarships.
Candidates for Scholarships must apply to the
Secretary of the County Council before the
1913, and the Council shall meet to consider applications
and award Scholarships, on
1913.

COUNTY SURVEYOR'S REPORT.
County Surveyor's Office,
Dundalk,
23rd January, 1913.
MR. CHAIRMAN AND GENTLEMEN,
As directed by the Finance Committee on Monday
last, I inspected the road at Clermont Bridge, at Heynes-
town. The road is flooded under the bridge owing to a,
gullet crossing the County road being broken down and
partly choked. The gullet carries the drainage of the
railway embankment, and appears to be a work con-
structed by the Railway Company, and should, I presume,
be maintained by them. I have reported the matter to
the resident engineer.
(2.) The road-roller was idle two days (13th and 14th
instant) owing to the driver being summoned to attend
Quarter Sessions as a witness. I asked the solicitor to
pay the hire and flagman's wages, 18s. 4d. I have not
received a reply.
(3.) | beg to recommend that consent be given by a
resolution to the erection of overhead Telegraphic lines
along the public road to the gate-lodge of Ballymscanlon
House, to the junction with the Dundalk Newry road, and
from the crossroads at Ravensdale, to Ravensdale lodge.
I am, Gentlemen,
Your obedient Servant,
J. M. C. LYONS.
Proposed by Mr. J. M. Johnson,
Seconded by Mr: Patrick Donegan, and
Resolved—That the Report of Mr. Lyons, Acting County
Surveyor, as now read, be adopted by this Council, and
that the matter relating to Heynestown bridge be referred
to our solicitor.

CORRESPONDENCE.
Office of Public Works, Dublin,
23rd December, 1912.
SIR,—In reply to your letter of 12th instant on the
subject of Gyles' Quay Coastguard Station, I am directed
by the Commissioners of Public Works to inform you
that the lifeboat-house was built entirely at the expense
of the Royal National Lifeboat Institution, and that the
Crown have had no interest in it. It is understood that
when the site of the Coastguard Station was surrendered
to the landlord by the Admiralty, arrangements were
made with the landlord by the Institution, for the future
occupation by them of the lifeboat house, which stands on
a portion of the Coastguard Station site.
It will be seen that the ownership of the lifeboat house
is a matter quite distinct from that of the boat-slip.
I am, Sir,
Your obedient Servant,
W. WILLIAMS,
The Secretary,
Secretary.
Louth County Council.
Francis Street, Dundalk,
2nd Januccny, 1913.
DEAR reference to the matter of Gyles'
Quay lifeboat house and the boat-slipg which was referred
to me on the report of the Harbour Constable, the Council
is doubtless aware that the Gyles Quay Coastguard
Station was closed, and surrendered by the Admirality to
7
the landlord, St. Clair E. M. Stobart, in the year 1905.
The boat-slip is not included in the premises so sur-
rendered, it was built at the joint expense of the Crown
and Royal National Lifeboat Institution about 1877.
Certain peir and harbour works, viz. :—A pier, consisting
of a wharf and stone parapet and sloping, escarpment, &c.,
with excavation of the harbour were executed by the
Commissioners of Public Works, under the sea Fisheries'
Act, and transferred to the Grand Jury in 1879, it does
not appear that the boat-slip in question was included in
the transfer, buf if the Council desire to have it trans-
ferred to them now, I am sure it can be done without any
delay. The lifeboat house was built entirely at the ex-
pense of the Royal National Lifeboart Institution, and the
Crown have no interest in it.
11 was understood when the site of the Coastguard
Station was surrendered to the landlord by the Admiralty,
arrangements were made with the landlord, by the Insti-
tution, for the future occupation by them of the lifeboat
house, which stands on a portion of the Coastguard Station
site.
Yours faithfully,
DANIEL O'CONNELL.
T. F. Filgate, Esq.,

am directed by the Local Government Board
for Ireland to acknowledge the receipt of your letter of

to advert to their letter of the 27th November last, from

although the Board could not accept all the items in the
Revenue account, still they consider it wiser to defer a
detailed examination of the figures, until the scheme has
been in operation for some time, and particulars of the
actual cost are available.

at their Adjourned Quarterly Meeting, held in the Council
Chamber, on Thursday, the 27th day of February, 1913.
Present
In the Chair—Peter Hughes, Esq., J.P.
Other Members—Patrick Callan, James Connolly, J.P.;

COUNTY SURVEYOR'S REPORT.
At the fourth (Adjourned) Quarterly Meeting on 27th
February, 1913.
Mr. Chairman and Gentlemen,
There are 49 Road Maintenance Contracts to be re•
newed from 1st April next—27 in Ardee District, and 22
in Drogheda District. Forty-six new contracts have been
made, subject to your approval ; 36 for a term of five
years and 10 (where the roads have been partly steam
rolled) for a term of three years.
The amount allowed in Ardee District for 27 contracts
was CI,543 6s. Sd. per annum, which was reduced to
Cl,408 As. lid. on tendering.
In Drogheda District the amount allowed was
C758 4s. Sd. per annum, which was reduced to
C(j47 3s. 2d. on tendering.
A steam roller clause has been inserted in the Speci-
fication on all important roads- now under consideration,
12
and contractors should understand that only the soundest
stone will be paid for. In some. instances inferior stone
has been supplied by contractors, and where detected,
money has been deferred or struct off. Good road metal
is the essential for good roads, and no amount of extra
labour will make a good road where soft material has been
laid down.
During the year the following contractor failed to carry
out his contract, which has been taken up by me in ac-
cordance with clause 21 of the Specification. I beg to
recommend that this contract be cancelled, and a new
contract is now applied for :—
No. 6, Philip Finlay, Dundalk to Drumconrath.
I am, Gentlemen,
Your obedient Servant,
JAMES DROMGOOLE,
Acting County Surveyor.
DUNDALK DISTRICT
COUNTY SURVEYOR'S REPORT.
At the fourth (Adjourned) Quarterly Meeting on 27th
February, 1913.
Mr. Chairman and Gentlemen,
I. The Dundalk Rural District Council propose to re-
new thirty-nine Road Maintenance contracts, nearly al I
of which have been set by contract, subject to your ap-
proval. Competition for contracts at the Adjourned
Meeting was quite up to the average, the amount at which
the roads were taken being eleven per cent. below the
amount allowed, and three per cent. less than the amoup
at which the same roads were set five years ago,
13
2. Applications for Special Works to the amount of
C948 IOS. Od. were also considered, but new works re-
presenting only C427 were passed. Of these the most
important is a Provisional Proposal for a new line of road
through Carrickaleagh, at an estimate cost of C360, which
is not to be formally considered now; the other works
are necessary road buildings.
3. The supply of road metal (trap rock) from the Bally-
makellet district, &c., to roads in the vicinity of Dundalk
has been rather protracted during the past season, and as
the quantity of this stone required for next October will
be greater, facilities for increasing the output from quarries
are most necossary. I beg to suggest that a stone-breaker
be purchased for this purpose.
4. Recent references in the press to the Road Board
funds show that about forty-five per cent. of the total
amount allocated to Ireland, up to March 31st last, has
been accepted. A re-distribution of the amounts allotted
to the several counties, and not claimed, is now proposed.
5. It is to be feared that the two years' allowance
marked for Louth, amounting to C2,400, may be diverted
and accepted by other counties unless immediate steps
are taken to qualify for a grant ; and with this object in
view, I would suggest that the Roads' Committee be
asked to take this matter up and formulate proposal, to
be prosecuted, and I hope, to successful issue.
6. | would also ask you to authorise the hire of a
second road-roller for Dundalk district for a few months.
It will entail no additional cost ; your solicitor to carry
out the legal formalities.
7. During the past year .ten road contractors have failed
to carry out their contracts, and the roads have been taken
up in accordance with clause No. 21 of the specification.
14

to recommend that the contracts be cancelled. New con-
tracts are now applied for : —

15
The following supplementary Report from the Acting
County Surveyor, Mr. John M. C. Lyons, was also sub-
mitted to tbe Council.
On Tuesday last, 25th instant, I found workmen exca-
vating for foundations of a new building, inside Carling-
ford Harbour.
The building, I understand, is being erected by Carling-
ford Lough Commissioners. I saw captain Doran, who
appears to be superintending the work, and he informs
me permission has been obtained from the Board of Trade.
The new building being inside your harbour, and ap-
propriating about 30 feet of a part of the pier, which
could be used by fishing boats, I beg to recommend that
your solicitor be instructed to take proceedings against
the Carlingford Lough Commissioners. Mr. \Vilson, of
Warrenpoint, is the contractor.
Proceedings have been instituted by order of the Finance
Committee, against the reputed owner of a new building,
inside 30 feet of the centre of the county road •at Blackrock.
The case Was entered for Friday last, but adjourned at
the request of the defendant's solicitor ; an undertaking
being given that work would cease pending the trial, but
the road contractor has reported that some work has been
done since.
I am, Gentlemen,
Your obedient Servant,
JOHN M. C. LYONS,
Acting County Surveyor
In deference to the request of Mr. C. Mac Mahon,
solicitor, the Chairman and Council proceeded to consider
the matter of the alleged encroachment of the house now
being built by Mrs. Walls, at Blackrock, which the Acting
16
County Surveyor, Mr. J. M. C. Lyons, states is not the
statutory 30 feet from the centre of the public road.
After considerable discussion it was decided to refer the
matter to the two Rural District Councillors representing
the district, and also to Mr. T. J. Byrne, J.P., the County
Councillor representing the Haggardstown Electoral Di-
vision, for consideration and report.
The following matters arising out of the Acting County
Surveyor's Report, Mr. J. M. C. Lyons, were referred to the
Finance Committe, and to the Council's solicitor.

CONSIDERATION OF ESTIMATE OF RATES IN
RESPECT OF THE ENSUING FINANCIAL
YEAR.
Before proceeding to discuss the annual Estimate of
Rates in respect of the ensuing financial year, the Chair-
mon read the following telegram from the Drogheda
Councilors :
Chairman, County Council,
Courthouse, Dundalk.
Unavoidably absent ; strongly protest against advance
on district rate — Donegan, Rath, Kelly, McCarthy,
McKeown—reduce threepence.
There was very considerable discussion over the matter,
and finally the Council were unanimous in passing the
following resolution :
Proposed by Mr. Bernard Hamill, J.P.,
Seconded by Mr. Patrick Callan, and
Resolved—That the Rates, as set out in the Secretary's
estimate in Drogheda and Dundalk, be reduced by 2d. in
the C, and by ld. in the in Ardee district, and that in
all other respects the estimate be adopted by the Council.

Council—

Finance Committee on the 17th February last, be, and is
hereby adopted by this Council, that is—allowing Mr.
Joseph Loudon the sum of $12 IOS. ()d. for the services
rendered by him.
CORRESPONDENCE.
The following letter from Dr. J. A. McQuillan, was
read.
February, 20th, 1913
DEAR SIR,—I would be glad if you please let me know
when the next meeting of the County Council takes place.
I would also like to remind you that I was provisionally
sanctioned by the Local Government Board, early in
November last, which sanction placed me under salary,
and at that time I had to appoint a substitute to do my
work while I was taking out the necessary course.
Dr. O'Connell has been doing the work since then, and
of course I will have to pay him for doing so.
I hope to take up duty about the 1st March ; hoping I
am not giving you too much trouble.
I remain,
Sincerely yours,
MCQUILLAN.
23
Proposed by Mr. Bernard Hamill, J.P.,
Seconded by Mr. Michael O'Meara, J.P„ and
Resolved—That we pay Dr. McQuillan up to the 31sU
December, in respect of his salary of C400 a year.

The Secretary's assistant, Mr. Finegan, applied for a
months salary to end of January. In view of his illness
and the fact that he was an insured person under the
Insurance Act, payment was not ordered.
A paragraph in the Auditor's Report relating to the
apportionments of the Returning Ofticer's Bill of Costs
for last County Council Election was considered. This
matter was postponed until next meeting of Committee,
Mr. C. J. M'Gahon, the Returning Oflicer, to be requested
to attend.
A deputation consisting of Messrs. T. Soraghan and P.
J. Murphy, two members of the Dundalk Rural District
Council, waited on the Committee and explained that a
building was in course of erection for Mrs. Walls,
along the public road and adjacent to the entrance to !
the beach, at Mr. Hamilton's Baths Blackrock. In view
of the fact that this new building was within 30 feet Of')
the centre of the public road, they asked that steps should
be taken to have the Law on the subject enforced. The
Committee resolved unanimously that the Acting County
Surveyor, and the County Solicitor, be directed to take
whatever legal proceedings were necessary to compel the
party offending to keep the buildings the statutory
distance from the centre of the public road.

To the Secretary Louth Co. Council.
Derrygalley, Moy, Co. Tyrone,
27th January, 1913.
Re Omeath Park and Mansion.
Dear Sir,
In the event of the Louth County Council deciding
to procure a Sanatorium for treatment of Consumptive
Patients in their own County. On behalf of the owners
of the above-named property, I beg to offer it as a very
suitable Pro ton object.
It stands in its own ground, of
about 60 Acres, and the House is in a commanding
position, half-a-mile from, and overlooking Warrenpoint,
and the Mourne mountains are in view. I should say
that there is not a healthier spot in the County Louth, or
more suited for lesidence of Consumptive Patients.
The House could easily be adapted to the purpose of a
Sanatormm.
Yours faithfully,
Lt. Col. G. S. Alexander.
The subject matter of the above letter was postponed.

Minutes of Proceedings of the Fiyrance Counnittee, at
their Meeting held in the Council Chamber, on Monday;
17th yanuary, 1913.
Present—
In the Chair-—Peter Hughes, Esq., J.P.
Other members—Patrick Byrne, J.P. ; Joseph H.
McArdle, C. A. Duffy, J.P.; William Woods, J.P.•, James
Connolly, J.P. ; James Wynne, Michael Meade, Patrick
O'Connell, Bernard Hamill, J.P., and Joseph M. Johnson,
Esquires.
The Acting Secretary submitted Accounts for Works in
charge of the Acting County Surveyor's and theb
Secretary's Offices, which on examination was passed by
the Committee. The Treasurer's authority amounted to
the sum of 7s Od.
The Annual Estimate of Rates in respect of the
Financial year ending 31st March, 1914, was submitted,
and after a short discussion it was ordered—" That the
Estimate of Rates be printed and circulated among the
members of the Council, with an explanatory table show-
ing the Increase of the Rates in each district and the
cause of same."
The Committee were unanimous in recommending an
increase of CIOO per annum, in respect to the County
Council contribution to the Louth County Infirmary.

CORRESPONDENCE.
Letter from the Department of Agriculture and Techni-
cal Instruction as under—
Sir,
15th February, 1913.
Adverting to previous correspondence I have to trans-
mit to you, herewith, for the consideration of the Louth
County Council, a plan of the proposed works of improve-
ment at Dunany. The geoyne will, no doubt, collect
sand, &c. under its lee, and would do so if it extended out
even twice as far as is shown in the plan. The Council
should realise this, as the berthage will need clearing from
time to time.
In connection with your letter of the 6th December
last, would you be good enough to let me know the time,
approximately, when the contribution of the Council will
be available.
I am, &c.,
T. P. GILL, Secretary.
To the Secretary, Louth Co. Council.
The following communication from Messrs. Sheridan &
Co., Was next read :—
Francis Street, Dundalk,
10th February, 1913.
Dear Sir,
We understand your Council have been in corres-
pondence with the Board of Works and the Life Boat
30
Institute, about the Life Boat House and slip, at Gyles
Quay, acquired by our Mr. Mackay as his personal estate
from the owner in Fee, and we have accordingly written
to the Board of Works, and also to the Life Boat Insti-
tute, requesting them to explain the alleged claim, we are
informed that a claim has been set up by one or either
of these Corporate Bodies to this property.
e have also been consulted by some of the Fishermen
from whom dues are sought to be collected by the
authority of the Council for the use of the Quay, and we
are to point out to you that if the County Council seek to
continue this levy and to make the fishermen pay any
dues whatsoever, they will have first to provide a proper
and safe anchorage for the Boats sheltering in the
Harbour, as we take it, even if the slip belonged to the
Council, and that they had the right to allow boats to be
hauled up over it to a place of safety, that a device of this
kind, would not comply with the requirements of the
Statute and the Bye-Laws as providing proper and safe
anchorage for the boats, in respect of which toll could be
collected.
We apprehend there are other difficulties the Council
will also have to face, but it is premature for us nos•, to
deal with same.
Yours faithfully,
SHERIDAN & Co.
The Secretary, County Council, Dundalk.

Letter from Mr. Joseph Loudon, as under :—
Dundalk,
17th February, 1912.
Mr. Chairmen & Gentlemen,
I beg leave to apply to you for some remuneration
for extra work done during Mr. Finegan's late illness, for
a long time before Mr. Finigan gave up, his health was in
such a precarious state that he was unable to perform his
portion of the office work, which fell into arrears ; to wipe
out this accumulation of work, I had to remain in the
office every night until ten o'clock.
I might mention for the guidance of the Committee,
that on a former occasion when Mr. Finigan was away
from the office ill, two clerks were appointed to do his
work at 30/- per week each, this time only one clerk was
appointed, this item alone saved an expenditure of
IOS. Od.
I have every confidence the Committee will recom-
mend to the County Council the amount they consider I
am justly entitled to, as they have always done with any
claim for extra work done for the Council.
Thanking you in anticipation,
I am, Mr. Chairman & Gentlemen,
Your obedient servant,
J. LOUDON.

Dear Sir,
The matter of the vacancy caused by the death of
Dr. J. J. Byrne, late Coroner for Drogheda, was on refer-
ence from the County Council, the subject of consideration
by the Finance Committee of this Council to-day, and I

appointment to this post rests with the Louth County
Council.

The following further communication from Sir William
J. Thompson, was next read—
21st February, 1913.
Dear Mr. Warren,
I find very often when .Patients return bome from
Peamount, that if they sleep in a shelter, they continue
the benefit they have derived from being at the
Sanatorium.
It has been reported to us that John Connolly and W.
Cormack, are willing to use shelters if they could get
them. The W.N.H.A. have shelters ready, and a County
Council has the .power to purchase them. I would feel
greatly obliged if you would be so kind as to find out
whether the County Louth County Council would supply
the shelves for these men. The Shelters cost C13 each,
this includes a bed, mattress, bolster and pillow.
Sorry to give you so much trouble, and thanking you in
anticipation.
Yours very truly,
WILLIAM J. THOMPSON.
To Valentine Warren, Esq.
In connection with the foregoing letter, the following
was also read—
Jocelyn Place,
March 13th, 1913.
Secretary, Co. Louth Insurance Committee.
Dear Sir,
I should feel obliged if you would kindly let me
know whether the County Insurance Committee or the
County Council have power (pending the forming of
Sanatorium and Care Committees, N,'hich I presume will
be formed here, as elsewhere, in connection with the
Tuberculosis Dispensary), to extend Home Treatinent,
viz. —Medicine and nourishment to non-insured persons
on their return from Sanatoria. - We have one such case
at present of a patient who spent 10 weeks at Newcastle,
paid for while there, by the local branch of the Women's
National Health Association.
Since the patient's return, they have not been able
(thro' Want of means), to procure the necessary medicine,
and n0t11'ishment required. Our Committee gave a small
sum Of money for this purpose, but unfortunately the
means at our disposal will not permit us to give any
further help at present, but we have another patient, who
will also require looking after in this respect.
Apologising for troubling you.
I am, yours faithfully,
N. M'GINITY,
Hon. sec., VV.N.H.S.

49
Local Government. Board,
Dublin, 19th March, 1913.
am directed by the Local Government Board
for Ireland to enclose herewith, for the information of the
County Council, the accompanying copy of the Tuber-
culosis Prevention (Ireland) Act, 1913, and to point out
that its provisions are intended to obviate certain diffi-
culties which have been experienced in connection with
the administration of Part 11 of the Tuberculosis Pre-
vention (Ireland) Act, 1908.
Section I and 2 relate to finance and remove the dis-
tinction drawn in the earlier Act in regard to the classi-
fication of establishment expenses and of patients' ex-
Penses. All expenses 'incurred by a County Council on
account of the treatment of tuberculosis over and above
the proportion defrayed from Public Funds as set out in
the Board's Circular of the 17th January last —8 M. 1913,
Miscellaneous—will in future be leviable over the entire
County area.
Provision is also made to authorise a County Council
to borrow money temporarily for defraying expenses for
e cnrrent financial year in respect of the treatment of
Tuberculosis, which may not have been contemplate at
the time when the estimates for the year were prepared.
Section 3 will enable Sanatoria established by a com-
bination of Sanitary Authorities under the Public Health
Acts to be acquired by County Councils in pursuance of
the Tuberculosis Prevention Act.
Section 4 enables County Councils who have established
Sanitoria to receive therein patients from other Counties,
and Will thus afford an alternative to combination between
county Councils through a Common Hospital and Joint
Committee of Management. Such cases will be dealt with
under agreements made in purscance of Section 4 (1) (b)
of the principal Act.
50
Section 5 permits the Local Goverment Board to place
any land vested in them at the disposal of a County
Council as a site for a Tuberculosis hospital or dispensary,
I am, Sir,
Your obedient Servant,
A. R. BARLAS,
Secretary.
The Secretary
of each County Council.
The Town Clerk

Letter from Local Government Board, 9th March, 1913,
referred to Dr. McQuillan for Report.
GENTLEMEN,
I beg to report on Che following Tubercular
Patient who is not insured—
John Cassidy, aged 36, Quay Labourer, 39, Mary St. No
Dundalk.
This patient was in Newcastle Sanitorium for 15 weeks'
is home from same since 8th March. He feels mucb
better than when he left home, und gained one stone
weight.
The house contains two small rooms and kitchen,
lives with wife and child, he has a room to self.
As he is very keen on sleeping out of doors, I would
suggest the provision of a, shelter for him, which could be
placed in his yard. The yard is IO yards by 4, the onlY
difficulty is shelter will not go through the door, unless
taken to very small portions.
I have the honour to be,
Your obedient servant,
J. A. MCQUILLAN

The Chairman drew attention to the fact that the Local
Government Board had not as yet taken any steps
towards furnishing the County Council with the names Of
of candidates qualified for the vacant post of County
Surveyor, although the names of applicants had been
forwarded to them so far back as November last, and the
Secretary was directed to write to the Local Government
Board and ascertain the cause of the delay.
The Secretary submitted Forms 22, for the districts o
Ardee, Drogheda and Dundalk, setting forth all paytnentS
to road contractors, as recommended by the three Rural
District Councils.—Payment recommended.
Proposed by Peter Hughes, Esq., J.P.
Seconded by Mr. Edward Goodman.
That we recommend the Council that new offices be
erected for the County Council as the present accommo-
dation for the growing business of the Council and its
committees is quite inadequate, and we request our
Secretary to communicate with the Board of Works as to
the terms and conditions for a loan for the purpose, an
also to ascertain from other County Councils who have
built offices for particulars and details regarding the
initiation and carrying out of the work.—UnanimouslY
adopted.

Letter from C. I). Oliver, Esq., Engineer of Department
Of Agriculture and Teehnieal Instruction, relative to Dunany
Pier :
ENGINEER'S OFFICE,
2 KILDARE PLACE,
DUBLIN,
16th April, 1913.
DUNANY PIER (JONES's QUAY) IMPROVEMENT.
DEAR SIR,
As requested by the Committee which I mef at Dunany
1
now forward a revised plan for the works there on the
lines laid down.
The reduction in the width of the berth - enables me to
provide for carrying out the quay woll to the full height
of the present pier for length of 75 feet beyond which the
base of a similar wall will be carried out for a further 35 feet
to a height of (i feet over the, excavated level.
This is probably as much as con be done for the money
available, but if we are able to save anything in the progress
of the work we can carry on the full height wall little further.
I am confident that thc wall designed is of ample strength.
It will be understood that. as I pointed out on the ground,
this wall must not be raiserl to its full height at once, but
It probably
brough up gradually as the, shingle aecutnulates.
will not be finished under two years, depending on the rate
of such accumulation.
Yours Faithfully,
CHARLES D. OLIVER.
Referred to the County Council.

SIR,
THE ROAD BOARD,
QUEEN ANNE'S CHAMBERS, BROADWAY,
WESTMINSTER, LONDON, S.W.
14ih April, 1913.
In reply to your letter of the 10th ultimo applying for
a grant to an expenditure of El 3S on surface tarring works
in the Dundalk Urban District, I am directed by the Board
to say that it is not their practice to make contributions
to works that have been carried out. but if the Urban
District Couneii are prepared to expend a further su of
$133 on approved works of surface tarring or tar macadam
and will submit details for approval, the Board wi!l consider
favourably the, question of making a grant of towards
the expenditure.
The Board are not prepared to make a grant or loan
towards an expenditure of €254!) on stemn rolling plant,
but if the County Council will borrow from other sources
the atnount required the Board will be prepared to make a
Yrant of El 100 and loan of 100 free of interest, repayable
five years, to an approved seheine of road crust itnprove-
work on the Lhlblin •Dundalk- Belfast Road. the Dundalk-
Armagh Road, or the Dundalk-Shereock Road.
If the County Council resolve to accept this offer par-
tic talars of the works of improvement with which they decide
should be forwu•ded to the Board for approval
In due course.

SUGGESTED RESOLUTION.
That, this Council is of opinion that, the present
torn of control in regard to the leading roads in tho
County under which all expenditure is a Eist: ict charge,
illould be amended in such a way as to bring main or
Imporant County roads under the scle control of tho
County Council, to be maintained as a charge cn the
nty-atrLarge.
That this centralization of management would tend
towards impro verneut in the roads compatible wyit,h
economy, and would also greatly facilitate negotat,ions
the Road Board, in that, the latter Body is em-
PGWered under the Development and Roads improvement,
Funds Act, 1909, to negotiate only as far as Ireland is
COUcerned with the Councils of Counties aud County
roughs.
That the Secretary be instructed to send a copy cf
this resolution to the Local Government Board.

Proposed by Mi'. .J oseph Dolan. Seconded by
Edward Goodman, and unanimously resolved —TIW
after due consideration we are thoroughly convinced
1st. • That the time, has now come when it becomes
imperative on the Government to abolish dual owner
ship of land in Ireland, thereby placing all occupiers
an equal footing.
tenanted arable land in Ireland fit for cultivation for
the purpose of re-distribution.
(A) To provide holdings for the 8,000 Evicted
Tenants and their 40,000 dependantS
23
not yet provided for, and securing to
them a full, fair, and final settlement
of this question, and thereby eradicate
the long existing grievances of this
deserving portion of the community
who are dleprive€l of t,heir homes,
their capital, and their livings through
no fault of their own, but because
they could not continue paying the
exorbitant rack-rents imposed upon
them, and seeing others enjoying
their old homes under more favour-
able conditions by recent legislation.
(B) To provide economie holdings for the
large number of young men now
leaving onr country to seek a living
in foreign climes.
'l'hat we urgently appeal to all I rish repre-
3rd.
Sentali&es who have the welfare of their Country at
heart to join together irrespective of party, to make this
Bill which will be the last Land Bill introduced into
the British Parliament a final and satisfactory settle-
ment of this long heart burning question.
4th. 'l'hat copies of this Resolution be forwarded
to the Prirne Minister of England, the. Lord Lieutenant
Of Ireland. the Chief Secretary of Ireland, the Leaders
Of the Irish National Parties, and the Leader of the
IJnionist Party.

Dear Sir,
I am directed by the Chairman of the Executive of
this Association to ask for your assistance in ourefforts
to secure the annual sum of E40,0()O promised by Mr,
Birrell for improving the condition of lay 'Secondary
Teachers in this Counti•y:
'I'he eonolitions attached to the, grant have been un-
reservedly accepted by all the Bodies representing the
Assistant Masters for the. improvernenf of whose status
the grant is avowedly given.
We feel that there is grave danger of the Grant being
withdrawn, and as the stun
at present available for
Secondary Education in Ireland is totally inadequate for
the growing needs of the, Country. the loss of this large
surn will be, nothing short of a national calamity.
We suggest that you should pass the Resolution ap-
pended, or one, of a silnilar nature.
We would point out that
this Grant is quite
independent of, and unconnected with the
grant for Scholarships.
I am, Dear Sir,
Faithfully yours,
P. C. GALLAGHER, Ilon. Sec.

The following letter from the Local Government
Board was read, and as the Committee were of opinion
that it did not effect the County Council, no order was
made on it :—
LOCAL GOVERNMENT BOARD, DUBLIN,
25th June, 1913.
SIR,
In connection with the establishment of County
Insurance Societies by County Councils under Sect. 81
of the National Insurance Act, 1911, tile Local Govern-
ment Board for Ireland deem it right to point out to
County Councils the steps which it is open to them to
take, in order to fulfil their duty of safeguarding the
interests of the ratepayers, in cases where they may be
required to give security to the Irish Insurance Com-
missioners by means of a charge upon the County Funds
to provide against any malversation or misappropriation
of moneys by the officers of such Societies.
The Board understand that the bond required to be
given by the County Council to the Insurance Com-
missioners provides that if a deficiency in the funds of
such Society is shown to arise wholly or partly from
such malversation or misappropriation the Com-
missioners shall be entitled to recover from the County
Council the amount of such deficiency, in so far as it
is not made good by the defaulting officer or other person.
The appointments of the officers of a County Society
are subject to the approval of the County Council, and
it would, therefore, be open to the County Council to
insist that each such officer should furnish a bond
of such amount and on such conditions as to prevent
the possibility of the County Funds being called upon to
meet any liability under the bond given by the County
Council to the Insurance Commissioners. In order
that the Council might themselves be in a position to
5
enforce the terms of the bonds of the officers, it should
be required that such bonds should in all cases be given
to the County Council either solely, or, if this course
is not practicable, to the County Council and County
Society (or Trustees) jointly and severally. The County
Council would do well to secure that the bonds of the
officers should be drawn in such form as to prevent
liability being repudiated on technical grounds.
The County Council might accordingly have the
bonds in each case examined by their Solicitor, special
regard being paid to the obligations on the County
Council, both as to amounts of security and otherwise,
undertaken in pursuance of the bond given by the County
Council to the Insurance Commissioners.
Provision should also be made for securing that the
bonds may be kept in force from time to time by the
payment of annual premiums or substitution of sureties,
as each case may require.
I am, Sir,
Vour obedient servant,
Further
letter from
as follows :—
LOCAL
SIR,
J. E. DEVLIN,
Assistant Secretary.
the Local Government Board,
GOVERNMENT BOARD, DUBLIN,
9th May, 1913.
I am directed by the Local Government Board for
Ireland to draw attention to the judgment recently
delivered by the Court of Appeal in the case of Weir v.
Fermanagh County Council and Enniskillen Rural
District Council, in which an important opinion has
been expressed as to the procedure to be followed by
6
County and Rural District Councils in regard to
proposals under the Local Government (Procedure of
Councils) Order, 1899, as amended. which involve the
borrowing of money for the execution of public works.
This opinion is to the effect that in such instances
it is mandatory on a rural district council in formulating
a proposal under Article 13 (2) of the Order to state
their decision as to the minimum number of years
within which the money to be borrowed shall be repaid,
and that if the rural district council omit to do so, the
County Council shall refer the proposal back to the
rural district council for amendment in this respect
before the proposal can be approved by them. The
opinion equally applies to proposals formulated by a
proposal committee of a County Council.
As the borrowing of money by county councils is
contingent on the sanction of the Local Government
Board being subsequently obtained, it will be necessary
that the number of years for repayment of the loan
proposed. by the rural district council (or proposal com-
mittee) and approved by the county council should not
exceed the period to which the Local Government Board
will be prepared to consent under Section 60 of the
Local Government (Ireland) Act, 1898 ; as otherwise
the requisite procedure for an amended proposal would
have to be carried out before the loan could be
sanctioned.
In order to prevent trouble to the councils and delay
in carrying out works, the Board deem it right, so far
as it it possible to do so, to apprise the councils of their
general practice as to the periods allowed by them for
the repayment of loans for carrying out proposals
affected by this opinion.
The large majority of such loans are for the purpose
of re-making roads by steam rolling, and in such cases
the period granted by the Board rarely exceeds five years,
it being, of course, understood that the specifications
and other arrangements for executing the works are
7
found satisfactory. The Board regret that they cannot,
owing to varying conditions, lay down any general rule
as to the period for repayment of loans required for
Other works, and in such cases they can only suggest
to the coullcils that in formulating and approving
proposals it should be specially borne in mind that if
they are to avoid carrying out the procedure a second
time they must insert a figure which would be certain
to be within the period likely to be sanctioned by the
Board, and on this point they should be guided by the
advice of the County Surveyor.
It would seem to be
open to a rural district council or proposal committee,
having inserted such number, to add to the proposal
qualifying words such as " or such greater number of
years as may be allowed by the Local Government
Board for the repayment of the loan."
I am, Sir,
Vour obedient servant,
A. R. BARLAS, Secretary.

NATIONAL HEALTH INSURANCE COMMISSION,
DUBLIN,
7th -July, 1913.
SIR,
I am directed by the National Health Insurance
Commission (Ireland) to refer you to the terms of the
proviso to Section 81 (8) of the National Insurance
Act, 1911, which enables them, where any part of the
cost of sanatorium benefit is defrayed by the Council
Of a County or County Borough, to increase the repre-
sentation of the Council on the Insurance Committee
formed for their administrative area and to make a
corresponding diminution in the representation of the
insured persons.
As your Council propose to defray part of the cost
of such benefit, the Commission have decided to increase
the representation of the Council on the County of
Louth Insurance Committee fronl eight members to
ten members. The Council have already appointed
eight menlbers, and these members will hold office until
the 15th July, 1914. It will, therefore, be necessary
for the Council at once to meet and appoint two additional
representatives, and the Commission would be glad if
you would take such steps to have such meeting con-
vened at an early date.
I am glad to add that the additional members so
appointed will also hold office tllltil the 15th July, 1914.
I am, Sir,
Vour obedient servant,
PIERCE KENT.

LOCAL GOVERNMENT BOARD, DUBLIN,
8th July, 1913.
SIR,
I am directed by the Local Government Board for
Ireland to acknowledge the receipt on the 4th inst. of
your letter intimating the resignation of the Secretary
to the Louth County Council, and, in reply, I am to
forward, for the information of the County Council,
the accompanying copy of the Board's Order of Decem-
ber, 1899, prescribing the qualifications for the position
of a Secretary to a County Council, together with a
copy of a circular letter setting forth the procedure to
be followed in making an appointment to the Office.
A form of queries in connection with the vacancy
is also enclosed.
By order of the Board,
E. W. LEACH.
12
GENERAL ORDER
Regulating the qualifications for the Offce of Secretary
of the Council of an Administrative County (not
being a County Borough) as constituted under the
Local Government (Ireland) Act, 1898.
To the Councils of the several Administrative Counties
(other than County Boroughs) constituted under the
Local Government (Ireland) Act, 1898 :
And to all others whom it may concern.
WHEREAS by Section 83 (1) of the Local Govern-
ment (Ireland) Act, 1898, it is provided that the Council
of a County other than a County Borough shall appoint
the Secretary of the Council (who shall act as Clerk of
the Council) :
AND WHEREAS by Sub-section (4) of the saip
Section it is provided that the said Secretary of the
Council shall have such qualificati011s (if any) as may be
prescribed.
AND WHEREAS we, the Local Government Board
for Ireland, deem it expedient to prescribe the qualifi-
cations of persons appointed to the said Office of
Secretary of a Council of a County other than a County
Borough.
i3
NOW, THEREFORE, we do hereby Order and
Direct that on and after the date of this Order no person
shall be eligible to be appointed to the Office of Secretary
Of a County Council in Ireland :
I. Unless he is over twenty-one years of age at
the date of his appointment ; and undertakes
to devote his entire time to the duties of his
office :
11. And unless he
(a) is an existing Secretary of a County Council
or has acted for a period of three years as a
duly authorised Assistant to a Secretary of a
County Council, •and satisfies the Local
Government Board that he has efficiently
discharged the duties of his Office :
or (b) unless he shall have been examined, and his
qualifications certified by some person or
persons appointed for that purpose by the
Local Government Board :
Provided that the Local Government
Board may, if they see fit, dispense with the
examination in the case of a candidate for
the Office of Secretary of a County Council,
who can produce a certificate of having
passed a public examination at least equivalent
in the opinion of the Board to that which the
Board require to be passed by Secretaries Of
County Councils, or can show that he possesses
actuarial, legal, or other special qualifications
for the Office of Secretary.
No person shall be appointed to the Office of Secretary
of the Council of a County who is unable to satisfy the
Local Government Board as to his age and character.
Sealed with our Seal this Eleventh day of
December, in the Vear of Our Lord One
Thousand Eight Hundred and Ninety-nine.
(Signed),
lö
LOCAL GOVERNMENT BOARD,
DUBLIN,
1899.
G. W. BAI,FOUR.
H. A. ROBINSON.
WM. L. MICKS.
R. BAGWELL.
SIR,
I am directed by the Local Government Board for
Ireland to state that their attention has been called to
the method of procedure adopted in some Counties 011
the occurrence of a vacancy in the oflice of Secretary,
and the Board desire to suggest, for the information
and guidance of the County Council, that, before pro-
ceeding to the making of a fresh appointment to this
office, the following preliminaries should be observed :—
In the first place, the County Council should fix the
amount of salary which it is proposed to allow for the
office in question, and should communicate their decision
to the Local Government Board, with a view to obtaining
the concurrence of the Board, as required by Section 83,
Sub-section 4, of the Local Government (Ireland) Act,
1898.
Advertisements should then be inserted in the local
newspapers, and in one or more of the Dublin daily
papers, inviting candidates for the position, and speci-
fying the amount of salary agreed upon, the limits of
age fixed, and particulars as to the latest date for sending
in applications and testimonials.
The Board would remind the County Council that
each member should receive a summons to attend the
meeting at which it is proposed to make the appoint-
ment three clear days at least beforehand, as prescribed
by Article 36 (10) VI. of the Schedule to the Local
Government (Application of Enactments) Order. 1898.
16
As their approval is necessary to this appointment,
the Local Government Board, on being informed cf
the existence of a vacancy, will forward to the offices
of the County Council a form of questions to be answered
respecting the candidate selected by the County Council.
I am, sir,
Vour obedient servant,
Secretary.
To The Secretary of the Co. Council.
