Letter and Statement re Blackrock Pier from Mr. J. H.
Murphy, Solicitor, Dundalk, dated the 16th inst., were
referred to the County Council for their sanction—viz. :
17, FRANCIS STREET, DUNDALK,
16th day of July, 1913.
Re BLACKROCK PIER.
DEAR SIR,
I am acting for a number of the inhabitants and
fishermen of Blackrock in connection with the above.
I enclose you copy of my letter to the Estates Com-
missioners and their reply in reference to the ownership
of the foreshore where it was proposed to make the Pier.
As I am quite sure the Board of Trade will grant the
necessary site for the proposed Pier and Roadway
thereto, I would ask your Council to put the matter in
hands at once.
I wollld suggest that your Council, through their
Solicitor, should communicate with the Department of
Agriculture and Technical Instruction and ask them
to send down their Engineer to make the necessary
plans and furnish an estimate for a Pier and Roadway
suitable to the requirements of the district.
Yours truly,
J. H. MURPHY.
21
Re BLACKROCK PIER.
Copy Correspondence with the Estates Commissioners.
17, FRANCIS STREET, DUNDALK,
14th day of March, 1913.
FORTESCUE ESTATE, COUNTY LOUTH.
DEAR SIR,
I am acting for the Fishermen of Blackrock Village,
County Louth, in connection with the construction of
a proposed Pier at Blackrock, County Louth, by the
Louth County Council. The site chosen for the pro-
posed Pier is on the foreshore adjacent to the holding
belonging to the late George Rogers, now in the pos-
session of his widow, Mrs. Elizabeth Rogers, situate in
the Townland of Haggardstown, County Louth, for
which a Purchase Agreement was signed by the late
George Rogers on the sale of the above estate under the
1903 Land Purchase (Ireland) Act.
The negotiations for the site for this Pier from Major
Fortescue to the Louth County Council were com-
menced as long ago as the year 1904, and Major Fortescue
the owner of the foreshore, would not give a site to the
Louth County Council for same.
Vou might kindly let me know will ther Estates Com-
missioners become the owners of the foreshore adjacent
to the holding of the late George Rogers, in the town-
22
land of Haggardstown, County Louth, when the sale
of the Fortescue Estate is sanctioned. Vou might
kindly let me know as soon as possible, as my clients
are anxious to get the County Council to construct the
proposed Pier at Blackrock if it is possible to obtain a
site for same from the Estates Commissioners, when the
sale of the above estate is sanctioned.
Yours truly,
J. H. MURPHY.

With reference to your letter of the 14th March last,
re proposed new Pier at Blackrock Village, County

LOCAL GOVERNMENT BOARD, DUBLIN,
16th July, 1913.
SIR,
With reference to the lists of arrears of rate in the
County of Louth for the period ended the 31st March,
1913, recently forwarded by you to this Department,
I am directed by the Local Governnlent Board for
Ireland to return the lists herewith, and to state that
owing to the unsatisfactory manner in which the
Collectors have discharged their duty and the lists have
been furnished to this Department, the Board regret
they cannot see their way to approve of the recom-
mendations of the County Council,• consequently any
payments they may decide to make in respect thereof
must be on their own responsibility and subject to
review by the Auditor.
The Board deem it well to set out generally the
irregularities which appear in the present lists and which
they regret to find have been more than once brought
under the notice of the Secretary of the County Council
but without avail.
25
Several of the amounts on the unused receipts do not
agree with the corresponding amounts on the lists of
arrears—e.g., J. Moonan, 1715 ; P. Cluskey, 1946, 2379,
etc.
It would appear that new forms of receipt have been
specially written out in a great many cases in order to
bring many of the items on the lists and corresponding
amounts on the receipts into harmony—e.g.,
P. M'Donne11,
J. J. Kelly,
3148
3151
3158, etc.
1926
2095, etc.
In these cases the amounts on the receipt forms now
for the first time furnished agree with the corresponding
amounts in column 12 on the lists, but for the reason
given above the Board cannot, in the absence of further
explanation, accept these documents as supporting the
claims of the Collectors for recoupment of irrecoverable
arrears.
On reference to the lists of John Woods and J. V.
Johnston, in which new receipt forms also have
apparently been made out . in many cases, it will be
found that the system mentioned in the preceding
paragraph has not been followed—e.g.,
J. Woods,
J. V. Johnston, .
4809
4860, etc.
730
731, etc.
26
In these instances it will be observed that the amounts
on the new form of receipt do not tally with the amounts
in the arrear lists, but represent apparently the amounts
actually received by the Collector, and it is only possible
to arrive at the amount in the lists by deducting the
amount on the new receipt form from the amount on the
original receipt.
The certified Summary of Arrears furnished by you
is also incorrect, and is returned ; a new list should be
made out correctly embodying the recommendations
of the County Council, and the annexed list should be
returned therewith.
Items appearing in column 13 of the lists (Refund
amount to Collector, but carry forward for subsequent
collection) cannot be legally so refunded, as the Col-
lectors did not close their collections by the 28th Feb.
It will be seen that no entry of these any)tlllts (column
13 in Lists) appears on the summary as furnished.
The Board cannot see why the Council propose to
strike off items 5381 on Collector J. Woods' Lists as
irrecoverable.
The Board must reqtlest that, in future, before the
lists of arrears are submitted to them that they may
be subjected to close scrutiny in the County office.
The system outlined by the Board in their letter
of the 5th June, 1912 (29604/1912) in cases of part
27
payment of rates should also be strictly followed in
future.
Six forms of Summary are also enclosed.
I am, Sir,
Your obedient Servant,
A. R. BARLAS, Secretary.

Arising out of the Report of the Acting County
Surveyors on the subject of Steam Rolling, the Com-
mittee made the following recommendation to the
County Council—viz. , That the terms offered by the
representative of Messrs. Edison, who was present,
be accepted—viz., That the hire to be 24/- per working
day for a six months contract, commencing about the
ISt October, 1913, with a standing charge during the
working season of 7/- per day ; the charge for the
Scarifier on Engine No. 189 be withdrawn, and no charge
during the idle season ; also that the Edison Company
be asked to supply an additional Roller or two, if found
necessary, at the same rate.
REPORT.
To THE FINANCE COMMITTEE LOUTH Co. COUNCIL,
July 21st, 1913.
MR. CHAIRMAN AND GENTLEMEN,
Steam Rolling of stone in the Rural Districts has been
finished, and no more stone will be available until
October ISt next when the annual measurements for
next year's stone will commence.
As you are now open to make new terms with the
Edison Company, Ltd., for the ensuing year, we would
suggest that a new agreement be entered into for a
term of say six months. A reduction in the cost of hire
might also be asked, and also that the charge for the
Scarifier attached to the Roller No. 189 be withdrawn.
30
Mr. Bartlett of the Edison Company, Ltd., is in
attendance to-day, and would be pleased to confer
with you should you so desire.
We are, Gentlemen,
Vour obedient Servants,
JOHN M. C. LYONS,
J AMES DROMGOOLE,
Acting Co. Surveyors.

.33
LOCAL GOVERNMENT BOARD, DUBLIN,
17th July, 1913.
SIR,
I am directed by the Local Government Board for
Ireland to acknowledge the receipt of your letters of
the 30th ultimo and 14th inst., relative to arrangements
for the treatment of Tuberculosis in Louth County ;
and, in reply to your inquiry on the subject, I am to
state that a Committee appointed under the provisions
of Section 5 of the Tuberculosis Prevention (Ireland)
Act, 1908, would have power to manage a Sanatorium
or Tuberculosis Dispensary established by the County
Council. The Council could not delegate to such Com-
mittee any powers other than those connected with the
management of the Sanatorium or Dispensary.
Pending the establishment of such institutions and
the appointment of a Committee of Management under
the above-mentioned Section, it is open to the County
Council under Article 36 of the Schedule to the Local
Government (Application of Enactments) Order, 1898,
to appoint a temporary Committee to take all the
preliminary steps as to inviting tenders and inspecting
sites, etc., with a view to making recommendations
respecting these matters to the County Council for their
approval.
I am, Sir,
Your obedient Servant,
A. R. BARLAS, Secretary.

CARLINGFORD HOTEL,

absence.

CARLINGFORD HOTEL,
CARLINGFORD, Co.
DEAR SIR,
Thanks for list of Sheep Owners received.
WARREN.
LOUTH,
18/7/'13.
As regards
my having any suggestions to make, representations have
been made to me here that it would hit some of the poor
owners very hard if they have to pay the regulation
amount twice in a period of eight days. A " small
owner can ill afford to pay, say, 5/- now and 5/- again
ill eight days time. More especially would it be hard
on the owners of healthy sheep. I think if your Council
could decide to give the second dipping free, or at least
at half price, it would be greatly appreciated. In this
connection it must be borne in mind that the ownerS
are also expected to assist in the dipping for which they
are paying.
Perhaps you will bring the Inatter before the Executive
Committee at Monday's meeting.
Vours faithfully,
W. ASHE KING.
DEAR SIR,
I wish to bring to your Committee's attention the
necessity of employing some men to assist M 'Kevitt
in the dipping. To - day when we were ready to
commence work there was none to dip, The sheep were
in most cases brought by decrepit old men, women and
children, none of whom could give any assistance. I
had, therefore, to employ four men at 4/- a day, as the
owners said if the dipping was not done to-day they
would not bring them again.
It is absolutely necessary that at least three men be
employed to assist M' Kevitt, otherwise we won't get
through over 100 per day, and it will take many months
to complete the dipping. I have arranged for three
men on Monday, as I really cannot get on with the work
without them.
In to-day's inspection of some 400 sheep I found
scab with three different owners.
Vours faithfully,
W. ASHE KING.

To the Secretary of the Louth Co. Council.
DEAR SIR,
Mr. King, V.S., was speaking to me to-day, and he is
not satisfied about one man doing the dipping ; he had
four in Co. Down. He is preparing to start on Saturday
morning. We had to look for a yard and can have one
from Miss Lee for two week's for the sum of LI.
Will you please let me know what can be done about
it.
Yours faithfullv,
W. WOODS.

The following comrmunication from the Department
of Agriculture and 'I'echnieal Instruction was next suli-
mitted to the Council, and a copy ordered to be sent to
each of the Veterinary Inspectors.
It is as under.
16th August, 1913.
Sir.
With reference to your letter of I inst., and the
entry on the subject in its enclosed copy of certain
Minutes of Proceedings of the Louth County Council, I
have to state that the Departinent observe that the
application of your Local Authority Veterinary In-
spectors (Messrs. Clinton, Maxwell and Donnelly) was
for increases of salary on aeeonnt of extra duties arising
out of the operations of the Tuberculosis (Ireland)
Order of 1913. "l'he Department assume, wherefore, that
the additions proposed to be made to the present salaries
of the Inspectors are in respect of such extra duties, and
they will be glad to learn on what basis these additions
(representing increases of from 250 to ;
per cent)
Irave been computed.
It is doubtless the case that the Order will impose
considerable extra work at first on the Inspectors, but
it is anticipated that as the operation of the Order pro-
ceeds the duty to be dealt with thereunder will
gradually decrease and finally become occasional only.
In these circumstances it seems to the Departinent
that large increases of salary on account of the Order
could not in any event be justified as a pel'lnanent,
arrangement.
I am, Sir,
Your obedient Servant,
P. GILL, Secretary.

Letter from the Department of Agriculture and 'I'echnica
Instruction, as under
23rd August, 1913.
Sir,
Adverting to your letter of the 7th instant, fowarding
copy of a resolution passed ht a meeting of the Louth
County Council on the saine date on the subject of the
expenses of the promotion cf the Provisional Order affecting
Annagassan Ilarbour, I have to state, for the information of
the Council, that the repartment regret that any misunder-
standing should have arisen concerning this matter; but
they desirc to direct the attention of the Ct uncil to the discus-
Sion which took place relative thereto at the meeting of the
Council, which was held on the 16th Novetnber 1911. Mr.
Green Chief Inspector of Fisheries, pointed out at this meet-
ing that it would not be possible for the I 'epartment to pay
costs in connection with the Provisional Order, and gave, as
one obvious reason. the possibility of the scheme being
opposed by parties in whom the Department were equally
30
interested. The suggestion that a Harbour Board should be
formed was made for the convenience and protection of the
County Council, and lest there Inight be any mistake in
regatd thereto, Mr. Green stated that, whether a Harbour
Board was formed or nct, the l)epartment were prepared to
make a grant towards the cost of the Harbour Works, if the
County Council were also willing to make a contribution.
'I'he I)epartment did, however, assist as far as was possible
in the preparation of the case made in favour of the
Provisional Order by having plans prepared and printed,
It may also
which involved them in considerable expense
be mentioned that, owing to the difficulty in finding a
suitable dumping ground for the material dredged from the
Harbour, the l)epartment's contribution towards the cost of
•the Works may eventually be greater than was originally
intended.
I am, etc ,
T. P. GILL, Secretary.
A copy of the foregoing letter was handed to Mr. Michael
Meade to be read at the next meeting of the Annagassan
Harbour Committee.

The County Councils of Meath and Louth havo

Experience has shown, as in the case of Dundalk
Infirmary, is likely to derive mucu
benefit from a Sanatorium established elsewhere.
There does to be any reason why botdl
CVjuncils should not agree Go allocate out to the Capital
sum available in each County for the building of

Lyogheda, and cut) of their annual income a sufficient

REPORT OF BLACKROCK PIER COMMITTFÆ.
Tho undermentionod Report from •dho Committee appoin-
ted to meet the Depart,rnenrt.'s Engineer ro tho proposed Pier
at Blackrock, was road by tho Secretary and approved of by
the Council : —
The Committee appointed by the Oounty Council to meet
9
the Engineer of the of Agriculture, met on
Tuesday, t'lv. 25th inst., when the following gentlemen at-
tended : —Very Rov Canon, Clarke, P.LP., •Haggardstown ;
Bernard ,llamill, J.P.; 1'. M.C.C.; J, M.
Johnson, M.C.C. ; E. Goodman, M.C.C,; Byrne,
J.P., M.C.C.; Thomas soran, U.D.C.; P. J, Murphy,
U.D.C.; Mathew White, B. J. H. Murphy, so-
]ioi/tor; D. O'Connell. Solicitor; and J. M. C. Lyons. Mr
Oliver, Engineer to tho Depa was present', and in
company with the Cmnmit.tee inspected tho sito of the pro-
posed Pier at •Blackrock. A num of Blackrock liehermen
and somo of Che prominent buyers of were present.
The Engineer discussecl the matter writth the fishermen as
to 'the most, desirable place to havo tho (Pier, and in com-
pany with thom, walked over the ground. Tho consensus of
opinion was that the leasi expensive and the most suitable
site would be immediately behind the Lifeboat House, where
a .süony patsago exists to the rivcw, marked by posts on
side, whetx. bhe lifeboat iB generally laumdhed. It was tjhø
unanimous opinion of the Committee, t.ho fishermen, and the
Engineer, that, the only ciffect'ivo pier would bo ono that,
would stand over high vat,er and afford a proper passage
for vehicll,es Prom the publiic road to the waior's edge, and
thus enable t,ho boats to bo unloaded direct, from the water.
ThJis would give tcho fishermen additional four hours at,
eaéh tide, which would be an extraordinary advantuago ; and
in• addition the pier would act as a breakwater, and have
the effect, of diverting bhe course 01 the river and expedite
t,he passage co deep water. The Wngir»eer also' •suggested
t,hat the beach on tho west side of the pier could be easily
made a good roadway for vehicular traffic, and on the water
side a safe b.eaeh for boats.
After a very exhaustive inspection and discussion with t,he
Committee and the fishermen, tho Engineer arranged to
make his report to the Department, and go {into the pro-
cost, €tnd promised bo bave this ready in about a fort-
night,.

RATE COLLECTORS.
The following letter was read ancl initialled by the
Chairman
Local Govermnent Board, Dublin,
8th December, 1913.
No, 64,552 | 1913.
Louth County.
Sir—The Locai Govern ment Board for Ireland have•
had before them your letter of the 2nd Instant relative•
to the collection of rates in Louth County, and, in reply,
the Board desire to point out [hat they do not agree in
the view expressed therein that "
the County Council
are in duty bound to pay the Rate Collectors their poun-
dage fees in respect of the half-year ended Sep-
tember, 1913."
It is obligatory on the Collectors to lodge the whole
of the first moiety of their collections on or before the
30th September in each year, and if they fail to do so,
they cannot be paid any poundage fees without the sanc-
tion of' the Board.
If, therefore, the County Council
dlirecd that Che Collectors should be paid poundage fees
without the Board's sanction, the members authorising
the payment would render themselves personally liable
to be surcharged by the Auditor.
The Board in their letter of the 7th October last set
out fully their reasons for refusing to extend the time
for the closing of the first half-year's collection in the
County. and [hey now (lil ect me to point ont illat Louth
was the only County in Ireland where the Collectors as
a body failed to close their collections on the 30th of
September, and that in the majority of the Counties all
the Collectors closed within the prescribed time.
In these circumstances the Local Government Board
are not prepared to re-consider their decision.
I am, Sir,
Your obedient Servant.
The Secretary
Louth Conn ty Council.
The followihg letters were
County Council :
A. R. BARLAS,
Secretary.

Copy Report on Omenth Park.
The Connnittee have inqilit•v as to the arnonnt of
arable and grazing land incliltletl in ()tneatll Park, angl as to
the annual value of satne.
'l'hey find that EIO to Æö() per
annum Inay be expected fro:n
annual lettin•rs of land ;
ill
addition, thc house, which is fine building antl in fair eon.
33
was let last year for for the summer months.
.1f put in thorough repair the Ilouse would eommnand a good
rent, or it might be let on repairing lease for a Ili"her figure
than above narned.
If the Council acquire the property, at even the outside
figure mentioned in the report of the Department's expert,
the annual returns from sub-letting would more than meet
the annual instalments under the Land Purchase Acts.
There would thus be no loss to the County Council on the
purchase.
'I'lle Cmnmittee have no means of checking the estimate
of value placed on standing timber, and would recommend
a valuation of both laud and timber by an expert, as a means
of arriving at a fair value of the property as it stands, and
as a basis of an offer for purchase.
As regards costs of maintenance, management and re-
planting, the Conunittee estimate that a figure of €200 to
±250 per annum would cover these costs, and would recom-
mend, if the Council decide on purchase, that application be
made for a grant from the Development Commissioners to
cover this.
If such a grant be obtainod there will be no
cost to the County, either on account of purchase or main-
tenanee, including re-planting, and the County will thus
acquire a property of considerable future value at a little or
no present cost. The timber eut on the park during the
past few years realised, we are informed, over El ,500.
With scientific planting and proper managerhent, the re-
established plantations should yield a very much higher figure
.after a period of years.
T. F. MCGAJ10N.
J. CONNOLLY.
WILLIAM BRENNAN.

rl'hc following letter from Sir Wm. J. Thompson, M.D.,
was read and initialled by the Chairman
Peamount,
Hazelhatch, Dublin,
19th December, 1913.
Dear Sir,
My attention has 'been drawn to a report of the County
Louth In•urance Committee in one of the Dundalk papers,
in which it is stated that the 12 County Louth beds are not
reserved for their patients.
I beg to say that this report is
not correct, as we can at any moment take in and acgommo-
date 12 patients from the County Louth.
Yours very truly,
WM. J. TIIOMFSON, M.D.

County Council,

WATER FOR ARDEE DIPPING TANK.
The following letter from Mr. Laurence Callan, Ardee,
for water supplied to Ardee tank was read and referred
to County Council
Ardee,
10th December, 1913.
Dear Sir—In reply to your letter of the, 9th Decem-
ber I beg to state since the sheep dipping commenced
in Ardee it was I did the work of carting the water, and
I had no complaint till- the present one. It was Messrs.
T. F. Filgate and J. Meade I arranged with at Is. 3d. per
The journey is twice as far now. as the sheep
load.
dipping tank was removed to the far end of the town.
1 should be looking for more money instead of being re-
duced, as I often had to take my horse from other work
and send it for one load of water, which was very in-
36
convenient to me. Therefore, I cannot accept any less
than the full amount of nuv bill Is. 3d.
Respectfully yours,
LAURENCE CALLAN.
The Secretary County Council.
The account referred to in above letter was for 65
loads of water at Is 3d per load—CT Is 3d.
OFFICE ACCOMMODATION.
The following communications from the County
Louth Insurance Committee and the Louth County
Committee of Agriculture were read and referred to the
County Council :
Insurance Committee for the Co, of Louth,
Clerk's Office, Dundalk,
21st December, 1913.
Dear Sir—I am directed by the Insurance Committee
for Louth to forward to the Louth County Council a
copy (as attached) of a resolution unanimously adopted
at their meeting held on the 17th lust.
Yours faithfully,
V. WARREN, Sec.
C. McAlester, Esq.,
Secretary Louth Co. Council.
C Resolution.]
That we again draw the attention of the Louth County
Council to the great existing need for County Ollices in
Dundalk ;
That owing to our duties being considerably increased
and will be further increased from the 15th January
next, we are now compelled to seek suitable perinanent
offices, as the rooms temporarily provided by the Council
in Earl Street are both inadequate and undesirable for a
County Public Body.
37
From inquiries made, it is now clear we must pay ar
very high rental for offices, although our income for ad-
ministrative purposes is very limited, to avail of a non-
recurring Treasury Grant of available up till the
12th January next, for furnishing and equipment :
That we thei•efore trust the County Council will lose
no time to have this important matter dealt with, in
order that the business of public bodies may be, con-
ducted under conditions in keeping with their positions.
Louth County Committee of Agricuiture.—ResoIution

STEAMROLLING OF ROADS.
The following resolution passed by the Clare County
Council was read, and referred to the County Council :—
(Cory.)
" That in view of the increasing demand for steam--
rolled roads, and the willingness of the Road Board to
co-operate financially and otherwise in the execution of
Approved Schemes, we consider the time has come when the
whole procedure in connection with proposals and the bor-
rowing of money under this heading should be considerably
sir.)plified and accelerated.
We accordingly req nest the Local Government Board
to take the earliest opportunity of amending the Local
Government (Procedure of Councils) Order 1899 in this
respect, so as to enable Rural District Councils at any
specially summoned Jneetincr to fornmlate and adopt pro-
posals for steamrolling of roads, and to empower County
Councils at any meeting thereafter to authorise the carrying
out of sante by contract or otherwise, due regard being had
to the recommendations of the Rural District Councils on the
several. proposals. "
Adopted.

arable and grazing land included in Omeat«h Park, and as to

figure mebt.ioned in the report of talle expert,

the annual instalments under t(he Laud Purchase Acts.

Mr, CLINTON'S REPOR'I'.
Dundalk,
31st Januarv, 1914.
The Secretary,
Louth County Council.
Sir,
I beg to report that the sheep was found on the
( :reat Northern Railwav (tonu)nnv's enlises affeeied with
seal) is of the disease.
As the owner is unknown
I have the anilnal detained on prenlises pending instruc-

MR. DONNELLY'S REPORT.
Drogheda,
9th January, 1914.
Gentlemen, — During the month of December, 1914, I
had five outbreaks of Sheep Scab in this district. The
animals in each case had been sent to Drogheda port
for shipment, and came either from the Dublin market or
County Dublin. I had the diseased sheep slaughtered
with the permission of the owner, and the incontact
animals treated. The restrictions in all cases have now
been withdrawn. There were two cases reported under
the Tuberculosis Order. I dealt with both these, and
furnished your Secretary with the necessary report on
the prescribed forms for each suspected case. No
other outbreak of contagious disease in animals oc-
curred during the month in this district.
Yours respectfully,
ANDREW DONNNLLY.
To the Louth County Council.
Executive Committee.

FOOT AND MOUTH DISEASE.
The following letter from the Department, dated 1st
February, No. D I, was read, and the Secretary was
directed to send copies to each of the Veterinary Inspectors,
with instructions to afford every assistance possible to the
Oflieers of the I)epartment, if called upon :—
Departlnent of Agriculture and
Technical Instruetion for Ireland,
(Veterinary Branch),
50 & 51 Upper Mount Street, Dublin,
1st February, 1914.
No. 1) 1.
FOOT AND MOUTH DISEASE.
The Department of Agriculture and Technical In-
*truetion for Ireland desire to brino• speciallv to the notice
of Loeal Authorities under the l)iseases of Anilnal.s' Aets
the fact that. Foot and Mouth 'Disease has appeared again
an having been at Naas,
in Ireland,
another, on this
County Kildare, on the 30th ulti11i0,
and
(late, at Ballv.sa.x, in the satne countv.
26
In these circumstances, so gravely affecting the inte-
rests of the live-stock trade, the Department trust that all
Local Authorities will instruct their Inspectors to exercise
special vigilance, and to impress on stockowners throughout
their districts the importance of like watchfulness, so as to
secure that there shall be no avoidable delay in recognising
any additional cases of the disease, should such unhappily
occur at other places, and reporting them forthwith as the
law requires.
With a view of facilitating the efforts for eradication
of the malady the Department also hope that your Local
Authority will instruct their Veterinary Officers to bc ready
to furnish expert assistance if called upon.
I am, Sir,
Your obedient Servant,
T. P. GILL,
Secretary.
The Clerk of each Local Authority

corsrrx LOUTH SCHOLARSHIP SCHEME, 1914.
Proposed by Mr AV m T Skeffington. J P ;
Seconded by Mr Michael O'Meara, J P ; and
Resolved—That the Louth County Council hereby
approve of the conditions of the Louth County Council
Scholarship Scheme for 1914-15 as follows : —
Two Scholarships of Æö() a year each, tenable at the
Dublin, or other College of the National University for
the Session, October, 1914, to June, 1915, and renew-
able for the two succeeding years, and a, fourth, if the
student follows a course in Agriculture, or in Indus-
trial or Commercial Science.
The conditions to be similar to those for the 191%-14
Scholarships Scheme.
The following is a copy of the Scheme :
OF LOUTH COUNTY COUNCIL
SCHOLARSHIP SCHEME, 1914- 15.
The Louth County Council offer two Scholarships of
a year each, tenable at the Dublin or other College
of the National University for the Session October,
1914, to June, 1915, and renewable for the two sue-
lows a course ill Agriculture, or in Industrial or Com-
mercial Seenee. The Scholarships will be open for Com
eial Science. The Scholarships will be open for Com-
betition to male and female Students who fulfil' the
following requii•ements :
(1) Whose parents or guardians are: and have been
resident in Co. Louth for the past three years.
(2) Who satisfy the County Council that they are un-
able of their own means to obtain a Univer-
sity Education.
8
(0) Who ha.ve been educated in Ireland.
(4) Who are in good health and character au.d under
19 years of age.
(5) Who pass in Irish and reach a standard of honours
in the aggregate of subjects in the Test Exam-
illation which gives proof of ability enough
to put a University Education to good use.
The Test Examination will be the Intermediate
Examination of 1914-—the order of merit being deter-
mined by the printed lilarks obtained by the applicants
in Senior Grade Honours as published ill the Official
Results List—a Pass in Irish being essential.
The Scholarships shall be awarded to the two eau-
didates best qualified in the judgment of the County
Council 011 consideration of merit ill this test examina-
tion, and of the fulfilment of the other requirements.
If either or both of the highest eligible competitors will
not accept Scholarships they shall be awarded to those
next ill merit, if qualified.
()ne or both Scholarships may be altogether withheld
if sufficient merit or need of assistance is not shown.
The Student accepting a Scholarship must pass the
Matriculation of the National University (unless the
University accept his Examination as
qualification) in 1914, and attend regularly the first
year's course in some facultv of the University, and
reside in a hostel or other residence approved of by the
County Council and the College authorities.
9
The College fees shall be paid direct Ly the County
Council to the College authorities ancl the hostel pro-
prietors, the balance, if any to be expended on books
and other expenses.
In order to retain a Seholarship for the second and
third year a Student must pass the College Examina-
tiou of that year at a standard of merit to be fixed by
the Council in eon-sulfation with the College authori,-
ties, and must also present a satisfactory report from
the College. as to attenda.nee at lectures and general
conduct. during the year.
The County Council shall have complete discretion
in applying or modifying these rules and in withhold-
ing or reduci,ng Scholarships.
Candidates for Scholarships must apply to the Seere-
t,ary of the County Council before the 15th September,
1914, and the Council shall meet to consider applica-
tions and award Sehoarships, at, end of September,
1914.
APPOINTMENT OF RETURNING OFFICER.
Before the applications received in response to the
advertisement were opened, the Secretary read the fol-
lowing letter received from Mr Francis P Joh nston,
Solicitor, Dundalk : —
Francis Street,
Dundalk,
25th February, 1914.
RETURNING OFFICERSTIIP.
Dear Sir,
I understand that for the position of Return-
10
ing Officer for the County Council Elections there •are
two applicants, Mr Patrek Roe and myself.
I leav'il.
however, that Mr Roe is not and wilil not be of age uptil
April next, and I take it the L G B would not sanction
the appointment of a person who is a minor on the
date of appointment.
I need hardly say Ihat person-
ally I have nothing whatever against Mr Roe, but
merely wish to point out that in my view he is not
qualified.
I presume the L G B have Imrdly tlaonght
it necessary to include this disqualification
usual circular, but it is elementary law that a minor
could not in, the first place enter into the necessary eon-
tracts pertaining to the duties of It which could be
made binding.
Your Solicitor, with whom Mr Roe served his ap-
prenticeship, will, I think, bear me out as to/ Mr Roe'*
age, and also as to the danger of appointing minor.
Should the Council in the circumstances still ailosv
Mr Roe's name to go forward, then I desire that m j
name be withdrawn.
Yours faithfully,
FRASCIS P
Charles M' Alester, Esq,
Secretary Louth Co. Council,
Dundalk.
The Chairman having requested the advice of the
Solicitor on the matter, Mr C) 'Connell! advised the Coun-
cil that Mr P J Roe had been an apprentice of his, and
would reach his majority by the 11th April, 1914, and
11
masmueh as that he would not be doing a,ny of the
actual duties of Returning Officer before that date,
that the Council could safely elect him to the position
of Returning ( )ffieer.
The following application was then read :
Francis Street,
Dundalk,
13th February, 1914.
Dear Sir,
Pursuant to your advertisement of the 7th inst,
I beg to make application for the position of Return-
ing ( )fficer at the forthcoming Local Government
Elections.
Yours faithfully,
PATRICK J ROE.
The Secretary,
Louth County Council,
Dundalk.
This being the only application before the meeting,
owing to Mr Johnston's withdrawal, the following re-
solution was then passed unanimously :

we hereby appoint Mr Patrick J
Itoe, Dundalk, as Returning Officer for the Election
of County and Rural District Councillors for the

sued.

MOTOR CAR LICENCE DUTIES.
Sir,
I am directed by the Local Government Board for Ireland
to state that it, has been brought to their notice that some
diversity of practice obtains •as to the enforcement of the
payment of motor car licence duties by Veterinary Surgeons
using motor cars for the purpose of t/heir profession, and I
am to acquaint the County Council that the Board are advised
that Veterinary Surgeons are not entitled to the abatement
under Section 86 (4) of the Finance (1909-10) Act, 1910. IHIe
Board have been informed tha•t the- Lords Commissionens of
his Majesty's Treasury take the same view.
The Boa:rd deem it well to communicate this decision to the
County and County Borough Councils in Ireland in order to
remove any doubt which may have hitherto existed on this
point.
The Seeretary of
The Town Clerk
I am, Sir,
Your obedient servant,
A. R. BARLAS,
Secretary.

LAMP AT CARLINGFORD HARBOUR.
Mr. Peter Woods, J.P., brought under tlhe notice of the
Commitfee the t,pouble experienced in connection with the
lighting of the new Lamp at Carlingford Pier. He stated
that the Harbour Master had extreme difficulty in lighting
the lamp, as it required to be taken out of the standard to
light and fill, and being an ordinary duplex lamp with an
29
open chimney, it was practically impossible to keep it lighted
w'hile it was being replaced in the frame.
Mr. Lyons, the acting County Surveyor, on being reque»sted
by the Chairman €0 explain the matter, stated that he had
procured this lamp speciallS and at considerable expense ; that
it was recommended specially for a Harbour light, and that
in his opinion Chere was no difficulty in the lighting of same.
A very lengthened discussion ensued •on the matter, in which
very conflicting views arose, but no order was made by the
Corn niittee.

CLERICAL STAFF IN THE SECRETARY'S OFFICE.
The qruxstion of the vacancy in the Council's offices was next
discussed, and it was the view of the Committee that the whole
question of the staffing of the offce should be put on a perma-
nant basis, so Chat the various departments of elme Council's
complex work should 'be done in a thoroughly efficient man-
nor. The various heads of the work were under review,
namely—The cheeking of the Rate Collectors' abstracts, the
ordinary •accountancy of the office, the correspondence, taking
and recording of minutes, road payments and county-at-large
payments, work under the Diseases of Animals Acts (including
work under S'heep Dipping Order and Cattle Tuberculosis Or-
der), Motor Licences •and Registrations and accounts there-
with, various and intricate returns for Recoupment Claims to
lhe several Goverllrnent Departments, special account keeping
in connection with t;he Tn berculosis Scheme, the details •of the
valuation work, etc; preparation of the complicated "leclyules
required by Act'S of Parliament, and Che general heavy work
in connection with the half-yearly audits under the Local Go-
vernment Board.
It was ultimately unanimously decided €0 recommend the
County Council to make t.'he following appointments, viz
Alr Joseph Louden, at a salary of CI IOS per week.
A Senior Clerk at a salary of C8() per annum, increasing to
Cl'2() maximum, by annual increments of C,5.
A Junior Clerk at a salary of C.50, increasing to C80 maxi-
mum, by annual increments of
That tho two latter appointments be made by competitive
examination, conducted, as in other counties, by the Local Go-
vernment Board, and that, in the case of tlhe Junior Clerik,
proficiency in Shorthand and Typewriting be compulsory.

Dear Sir,
I notice in the peport of a recent meeting of t"IiO
County Council that it is proposed to hold a competition
among Architects for the design of the Council's new offices.
T a.m instructed send you the enclcsed regulations for ar-
ehit,ectural competitions issued by the Royal Institute of the
Architects of Ireland, Which may be of assistance to the Coun-
eil in such conditions and instructions as will
aexsgptable to Architects of good standi ng, and thus ensure
fulier and better competition.
My Institute will be very pleased to give further ptuiiculars
or any otther assistance in drawing up instructions and condi-
tions on lines equitable to the Council and our profession.
Yours faithfully,
FREDK. G. HICKS,

Regulations of the Royal Institute of the Architects of Ireland
For Architectural Competitions.
It is assumed that Che object of the Promoters is to obtain
the best design for the purpose in view. This object may best
be secured by conducting all Competitions upon the lines laid
down in Che following Regulations, which aave been framed
with a view to securing the lyst results to the Promoters with
scrupulous fairness to the competitors.
Members of the Royal Institute, of the Architects of Ireland
and Allied Societies not compete excepting under conditions
based •on these Regulations.
The Condi Lions of a Competition shall contain the follow Re-
gulations (A) to (F) as essential :—
(A) There shall be appointed for every Competition one
.or more fully qualified professional Assessors, to whom
the Whole •of the designs shall be submitted.
(B) No Promoter of a Competition, and no Assessor en-
gaged upon it, nor any employee of either, shall eom-
pete or Assist Competitor, or act as Architect, or
joint Architect, for the proposed work.
(C) Each design shall be accompanied by a declaration,
signed by the eompe€itor, or joint competitors, stating
that. 'the design is his or their own personal work, and
that the drawings have been prepared under his or
their own supervision. A successful competitor must
be prepared to satisfy the Assessor that he is the bona-
fide author of the design he has submitted.
(D') The premiums Shall be yaid in accordance with the
Assessor's award, and the author of the design placed
first by the Assessor shall be employed to carry out the
work, unless the Assessor shall be satisfied that there
is some valid objection €0 such employment, in which
case the author of the design placed next in order of
merit Shall be eloployed, subject to a similar condition.
The award of the Assessor 'hall not be set aside for
any other reason.
(E) If no insttuetions given to the author of thc, de-
sign selected by the Assessor to proceed within twelve
months from the date of the award' then 'he shall re-
oeive payment for his services in connection with
preparation of the Competition (Irmving.s of a sum
to 11 wer cent on the amount of estimated
cost. If the work is subsequently proceeded with, the
11 cent previcusly paill Vo him shall form part of
his ultimate commission.
(P) IPho selected Architect shall Ibe paid in accordance
with the Schedule of Charges sanctioned and pub.
lislwtl by the Royml Institute of the Architects of Ire-
land.
I.—Tlre Promoters of an intended Competition should, as their
first step, appoint one or more professional Assessons, ar-
ehitects of established rcputation, whose appointment
kbe publis/hed in the original advertisements and in-
structions. The selection of an Assessor should be made
with the greatest possible care, as the successful result of
Competition will depend very largly upon his exper-
ie.nce and ability.
The President Of the Royal Institute of the Architects of
Ireland is always prepared €0 act as honorary advised to
Promote•s in their appointment of Assessors.
2. —The duties of an Assessor are as follows
(a) To confer and advise the Promoters on their
requirements, and on the questions of cost and pre-
miums to be offered.
(b) To draw up instructions for tkhe guidance of eompe—
titors, and for the conduct of the Competition, incor-
porating the whole of the clauses of these Regulations
which are applicable to tlle particular Competition.
NOTE. —It is essential, in drawing lt.p the Instructions,
to state definitely which of the conditions must be .<rictly
adhered to, undt penalty of disqualification from the
Competition, and which of them are optional.
(e) To answer queries raised by competitors within a li-
mitecl time during the preparation of the designs, such
answers to be sent to all competitors.
(d) To examine all the designs su brnitted 'by competitors,
and 60 determine whether they conform to the condi-
tions, and to exclude any wlt;eh do not.
(e) To report to the Promoters on the designs not so ex-
eluded, and to award the premiums in strict adherence
to the Conditions.
3. —Compptit,ians may be conducted in one of the following
ways : —
(a) By advertisement. inviting architects willing to corn-
for the intended work to send in designs.
For
Competitions for pubdie works of great architectural
importance this method is recommended.
(b) By advertisement. inviting architects willing to com-
pete for the intended work to send in their names by
a given day, with suoh other information as they may
think likely to advance their claims to be admitted to
the Competition. From these names the Promoters,
with the advice of the Assessor, shall select a limited
number to compete, and each competitor thus selected
shall receive a specified sum for the preparation of his
design.
(c) By personal invitation to a limited ntnnber of selec-
ted arc)hitects to join in n competition for the inten-
ded work. Each competitor shall receive a specified
sum for the preparation of his design.
NOTE —Where a deposit is required for supplying the
4.
37
Instructions, it shall be returned on the receipt of a bona-
fide design, or if the appliea,nt cleclines to compete, and re-
turns the said Instructions Mlitllin a month after the re-
ceipt of replies to competitors' questions.
The number, scale, and method of finishing of the twqn:ired
drawings shall be distinctly set forth. The drawings shall
not be more in number or to a larger scale than necessary
clearly to explain the design, and such drawings shall be
u niform in size, number, mode of colouring ,ancl mount-
i.ng. As a general rule, a scale of 16 feet to 1 inch Mi 11 be
found sufficient for plans, sections, and elevations, or in
Che case of very large buildings a smaller scale might
suffice.
Unless the Assessor advises that perspective drawings are de-
sira ble, tåhe.v shall not be admitted.
6. —No design shall bear any motto or distinguishing mark ;
but all designs shall be numbered by the Promoters in or-
der of receipt.
(j.—A design shall be excluded from a Competition—
(a) If sent in after the period nanted (accidents in tran-
sit excepted) ;
(b) If it does not give substantially the accommodation
asked for ;
(c) If it exceeds the limits of site as shown on t,he plan
issued by the Promoters, the figured dimensions on
which shall be adhered to ;
(d) If the Assessor shall determine that its probable cost
will exceed by IO per cent the outlay stated in tlhe In-
structions, or the estimate of the competitor should
no outlay be stated. If the Assessor be of opinion
that t,'he outlay stated in the Instructions is inade-
quat.e, the shall not be bound in tho selection of a de-
sign by the amount named in such Instructions, but
the question of cost shall nevertheless be a material
element in the consideration of the award;
38
(e) If any of tho Conditions or Instructions other than
those of a suggestive Oharacter are violated ;
(f) If a competitor gjnall disclose his identity or attempt
to influence the decision.
7.—All designs ancl reports submitted in a Competition for a
public building, except any excluded uncler •Cla,use G, shall
publicly exllibit,ed after the award has been made,
which award shall be published at t'he time of exhibition;
ancl all designs and reports submitted in a competition for
a, v,rivate 'building shall be similarly exhibited to the eom-
petitors.
8.—All drawings su'bmitted in a competition, except those of
the design selected to be carried out, shall be returned to
competitors.
The usual R.I.A.I. Seale of Charges for Ass€nssing Competi-
tions, whether •by jury or otherwise, is the sum of Thirty
Guineas, plus one-fifth per cent upon the estimated cost of 'the
proposed building.
F. G. mcKs,
Hon. Secretary R.I.A.I.
The Royal Institute of the Architects of Ireland,
31 South Frederick Street, Dublin.
POOR RATE COLLECTION.
following letter was road, and the Secretary was directed
to send copy to each of the Poor Rate Collectors :—
Local Government Board,
Dublin, March, 1914.
No. 11122.
Louth County.
Sir,
With reference to the collection of the Poor Rate in
Louth County for the current half-year, I am directed by the
Local Government Board for Ireland to state that they have
received a report from their Inspector, Mr E A Saunderson,
39
from they note that at this advanced stage of the col-
lection (the 28th ultimo), only out of a total of C16,368
had been collected.
The Collectors are, no doubt, aware that the question of the
extent to which their remuneration in respect of last half-
year's collection eau •be paid, remains undecided, and their
failure Io proceed diligently with their collections in the pre-
sent half-year must be regarded as seriously prejudicing tmeir
claim to lenient. treatment.
The Board have noted especially that the following Collectors
are extremey backward in their collections Hughes,
Veter M.Donnell, Patrick Donegan, John Kelly and Patrick
Cluskey.
I am €0 add that ait the elose Of the year the Board will be
compelled to cofisider whether Collector Hughes can be allowed
to retain his position.
I am, Sir,
Your obedient Servant,
A. R. BARLAS,
Secretary.
